\widetilde{\operatorname{Hilb}}^{d}(S)
is defined to be the
reduced fiber product, see Subsection
1.7

\textstyle{\widetilde{\operatorname{Hilb}}^{d}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{red}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hilb}^{d}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S^{\times d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sym}^{d}(S).}

By the result of Haiman
[
19
]
, the map
\psi
is a flat finite map
of degree
d!
, and there is an
\mathfrak{S}_{d}
-action on

\widetilde{\operatorname{Hilb}}^{d}(S)
. Then the object

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{E}^{d}:=(\psi_{*}\eta^{*}(E^{\boxtimes d}))^{\mathrm{sign}}\otimes\det\mathcal{A}^{-1}\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\operatorname{Hilb}^{d}(S))

is a Cohen–Macaulay sheaf supported on the closed subscheme

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hilb}^{d}(\mathcal{C}_{b})\subset\operatorname{Hilb}^{d}(S),

where
\mathcal{C}_{b}\subset S

is the spectral curve
of
E
.
Here
\det\mathcal{A}
is a line bundle on
\operatorname{Hilb}^{d}(S)
whose fiber
at
Z\subset S
is
\det\chi(\mathcal{O}_{Z})
, and
(-)^{\mathrm{sign}}

stands for the anti-invariant part with respect to
the action of
\mathfrak{S}_{d}
.
Then for each quasi-compact open substack

U\subset\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}
,
after applying the isomorphism given by

\displaystyle\phi_{L}\colon\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}},\ (-)\mapsto(-)\otimes L

for a sufficiently ample line bundle
L
on
C
,
the sheaf
\mathcal{P}_{E}|_{\phi_{L}(U)}
is a
descendant of
\mathcal{Q}_{E}^{d}
for
d\gg 0
by the Abel Jacobi map

\displaystyle\mathrm{AJ}\colon\operatorname{Hilb}^{d}(\mathcal{C}/\mathcal{B})\to\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}.

Here
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{B}
is the universal spectral curve
and
\mathrm{AJ}
sends
Z\subset\mathcal{C}_{b}
to
the dual of
I_{Z}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}_{b}}
.
We refer to
[
6
, Section 4.1]
,
[
27
, Section 3.2]
for more details.

Using the above explicit construction of
\mathcal{P}_{E}
, we show that

\mathcal{P}_{E}
satisfies the following Hecke eigen property:

Proposition 3.7
.

The sheaf
\mathcal{P}_{E}\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}})
satisfies
that

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{\vee}(\mathcal{P}_{E})\cong\mathcal{P}_{E}\boxtimes E.

Proof.

We define the derived stack over
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}

\displaystyle p^{{\dagger}}\colon\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}{\dagger}}\to\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}

which classifies pairs

\displaystyle(E,s),\ E\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(S),\ s\colon\mathcal{O}_{S}\to E

where
E
is compactly supported pure one-dimensional sheaf with support
\mathcal{C}_{b}
for
b\in\mathcal{B}
and
s
is
generically surjective. The map
p^{{\dagger}}
forgets the section
s
.
Its classical truncation is nothing but the moduli space of
Pandharipande-Thomas stable pairs
[
36
]
on
S
. Therefore by
[
37
, Proposition B.8]
,
we have

\displaystyle(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}{\dagger}})^{\mathrm{cl}}\cong\bigcup_{d\geqslant 0}\operatorname{Hilb}^{d}(\mathcal{C}/\mathcal{B}).

We consider the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}{\dagger}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{{\dagger}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{H}ecke^{\mathrm{greg}{\dagger}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{{\dagger}},\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{{\dagger}})}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ev}_{2}^{{\dagger}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}{\dagger}}\times S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{{\dagger}}\times\operatorname{id}_{S}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}ecke^{\mathrm{greg}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathrm{ev}_{1},\mathrm{ev}_{3})}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ev}_{2}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}\times S}

Here
\mathcal{H}ecke^{\mathrm{greg}{\dagger}}

is defined by the right Cartesian square, and it classifies the diagrams

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{x}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Here the bottom sequence is as in (
3.3
) and each vertical
arrows are generically surjective.
The right square in (
3.5
) is Cartesian but the left square in (
3.5
) is not Cartesian.

Let

\displaystyle\overline{S}:=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{O}_{C}\oplus\Omega_{C})\to C

be the projective compactification of
S
. There is a morphism

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}{\dagger}}\to\mathbf{Hilb}(\overline{S})=\bigcup_{d\geqslant 0}\mathbf{Hilb}^{d}(\overline{S})

where
\mathbf{Hilb}^{d}(\overline{S})
is the derived
moduli stack of ideal sheaves
I_{Z}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\overline{S}}
of zero-dimensional subscheme

Z\subset\overline{S}
with length
d
.
The above morphism is given by

\displaystyle(\mathcal{O}_{S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s}}{{\to}}E)\mapsto\mathbb{D}_{\overline{S}}(I)\otimes\det I

where
I=(\mathcal{O}_{\overline{S}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s}}{{\to}}E)
is the two term
complex on
\overline{S}
, and
\mathbb{D}_{\overline{S}}(I)
is its derived dual.
For a closed subscheme
Z\subset S
of dimension zero, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ext}_{\overline{S}}^{2}(I_{Z},I_{Z})=\operatorname{Hom}(I_{Z},I_{Z}\otimes\omega_{\overline{S}})=H^{0}(\omega_{\overline{S}})=0.

Therefore
\mathbf{Hilb}(\overline{S})
is classical, i.e.

\displaystyle\mathbf{Hilb}(\overline{S})=\operatorname{Hilb}(\overline{S}).

Moreover there is a factorization

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}{\dagger}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{B}\times\operatorname{Hilb}(S)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{B}\times\operatorname{Hilb}(\overline{S})

where the first arrow is a closed immersion and the second arrow is an open immersion.

Similarly let
\mathbf{Hilb}^{d,d-1}(\overline{S})
be the
derived moduli stack which classifies exact sequences

(3.49)

\displaystyle 0\to I_{Z_{2}}\to I_{Z_{1}}\to\mathcal{O}_{x}\to 0

where
Z_{1}\subset Z_{2}\subset\overline{S}
are zero-dimensional subschemes with
length
d-1
,
d
, respectively. Its classical truncation
has the expected dimension
2d
by
[
19
, Proposition 3.5.2]
, therefore

\mathbf{Hilb}^{d,d-1}(\overline{S})
is also classical

\displaystyle\mathbf{Hilb}^{d,d-1}(\overline{S})=\mathrm{Hilb}^{d,d-1}(\overline{S})

where the right-hand side is the classical nested Hilbert
scheme which parametrizes zero-dimensional subschemes
Z_{1}\subset Z_{2}\subset\overline{S}
. Let

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hilb}^{d,d-1}(S)\subset\operatorname{Hilb}^{d,d-1}(\overline{S})

be the open subscheme corresponding to
Z_{1}\subset Z_{2}\subset S
, and
set

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hilb}^{\mathrm{nest}}(S):=\bigcup_{d\geqslant 1}\operatorname{Hilb}^{d,d-1}(S).

We have the evaluation morphisms

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hilb}^{d}(S)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{ev}_{2}^{\mathrm{H}}}}{{\leftarrow}}\operatorname{Hilb}^{d,d-1}(S)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{\mathrm{H}},\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\mathrm{H}})}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Hilb}^{d-1}(S)\times S.

By taking the dual of the sequence (
3.5
), we obtain

(3.50)

\displaystyle 0\to\mathbb{D}_{\overline{S}}(P_{2})\otimes L\to\mathbb{D}_{\overline{S}}(P_{1})\otimes L\to\mathcal{O}_{x}\to 0

where
P_{i}
and
L
are given by

\displaystyle P_{i}=(\mathcal{O}_{\overline{S}}\to E_{i})\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\overline{S}),\ L=\det P_{1}=\det P_{2}.

The sequence (
3.50
) is of the form (
3.49
), and
the above construction induces the closed immersion

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}ecke^{\mathrm{greg}{\dagger}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{B}\times\operatorname{Hilb}^{\mathrm{nest}}(S)

such that there is a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{B}\times\operatorname{Hilb}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}\times\operatorname{Hilb}^{\mathrm{nest}}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{\mathrm{H}},\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\mathrm{H}})}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ev}_{2}^{\mathrm{H}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}\times\operatorname{Hilb}(S)\times S}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}{\dagger}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}ecke^{\mathrm{greg}{\dagger}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{{\dagger}},\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{{\dagger}})}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ev}_{2}^{{\dagger}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}{\dagger}}\times S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i\times\operatorname{id}_{S}}

Here the vertical arrows are closed immersions and the left square is Cartesian (since
Z_{2}\subset\mathcal{C}_{b}
implies
Z_{1}\subset\mathcal{C}_{b}
), but the right square is not.

From the following diagram

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p^{{\dagger}}}}{{\leftarrow}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}{\dagger}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i}}{{\hookrightarrow}}\mathcal{B}\times\operatorname{Hilb}(S)

we define the following object

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{E}:=i_{*}(p^{{\dagger}})^{*}\mathcal{P}_{E}\in\operatorname{Coh}(\{b\}\times\operatorname{Hilb}(S)).

Here
b\in\mathcal{B}
corresponds to the spectral curve of
E
.
We denote by

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{E}^{d}\in\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Hilb}^{d}(S))

the component of
\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{E}
. By the construction of

\mathcal{P}_{E}
as a descent of
\mathcal{Q}_{E}^{d}
for
d\gg 0
, we have
\mathcal{Q}_{E}^{d}=\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{E}^{d}
for
d\gg 0
.

Let
\mathrm{H}^{\vee}_{\operatorname{Hilb}}
be the functor

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{\vee}_{\operatorname{Hilb}}\colon\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Hilb}(S))\to\operatorname{Coh}(\operatorname{Hilb}(S))\otimes\operatorname{Coh}(S)

defined by

\displaystyle(-)\mapsto(\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{\mathrm{H}},\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\mathrm{H}})_{*}(\mathrm{ev}_{2}^{\mathrm{H}})^{*}(-).

Then by the diagrams (
3.5
), (
3.5
) and base change, we have

\displaystyle(i\times\operatorname{id}_{S})_{*}(p^{{\dagger}}\times\operatorname{id}_{S})^{*}\mathrm{H}^{\vee}(\mathcal{P}_{E})\cong\mathrm{H}^{\vee}_{\operatorname{Hilb}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{E}).

Therefore by the uniqueness of descent, the proposition follows from Lemma
3.8

∎

Lemma 3.8
.

There is an isomorphism

(3.55)

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}_{\operatorname{Hilb}}^{\vee}(\mathcal{Q}_{E}^{d})\cong\mathcal{Q}_{E}^{d-1}\boxtimes E.

Proof.

We define
W^{d}
and
V^{d}
by the Cartesian squares

\textstyle{\widetilde{\operatorname{Hilb}}^{d}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\textstyle{W^{d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V^{d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\operatorname{Hilb}}^{d-1}(S)\times S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hilb}^{d}(S)}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hilb}^{d,d-1}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hilb}^{d,d-1}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hilb}^{d-1}(S)\times S.}

Since
\widetilde{\operatorname{Hilb}}^{d}(S)\to\operatorname{Hilb}^{d}(S)
is finite flat,
\widetilde{\operatorname{Hilb}}^{d}(S)
is reduced and
the derived fiber products
W^{d},V^{d}
are generically reduced, they are classical reduced schemes, i.e.

\displaystyle W^{d}=(W^{d})^{\mathrm{cl,red}},\ V^{d}=(V^{d})^{\mathrm{cl,red}}.

It follows that we have the following reduced Cartesian squares

\textstyle{W^{d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{red}}
\textstyle{T^{d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{red}}
\textstyle{S^{\times d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hilb}^{d,d-1}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sym}^{d-1}(S)\times S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sym}^{d}(S).}

The right bottom arrow is the addition map, and
T^{d}
is defined
by the above right square.

We have the decomposition of
T^{d}
into
irreducible components

\displaystyle T^{d}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{d}T^{d}_{i}

where each component
T_{i}^{d}
is isomorphic to
S^{\times d}
.
The
\mathfrak{S}_{d}
-action on
S^{\times d}

given by
\sigma(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})=(x_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,x_{\sigma(d)})

lifts to the action on
T^{d}
.
Let
\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{d}\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}
be elements such that
\sigma_{i}(d-i+1)=d
.
Then each
T_{i}^{d}
is given by the commutative diagram

\textstyle{S^{\times d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{i}}
\textstyle{S^{\times d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sym}^{d-1}(S)\times S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sym}^{d}(S).}

Here the left vertical arrow is

\displaystyle(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})\mapsto\left(\sum_{j\neq d-i+1}x_{j},x_{d-i+1}\right).

Namely the diagram (
3.5
)
induces the closed immersion
S^{\times d}\hookrightarrow T^{d}

whose image is
T_{i}^{d}
. Then
it follows that
T_{i}^{d}=\sigma_{i}(T_{1}^{d})
.

By the diagram (
3.5
), the
\mathfrak{S}_{d}
-action on
T^{d}
further lifts
to an action on
W^{d}
such that

\displaystyle W^{d}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{d}W_{i}^{d},\ W_{1}^{d}=V^{d},\ W_{i}^{d}=\sigma_{i}(W_{1}^{d}).

Here each
W_{i}^{d}
is an irreducible component of
W^{d}
.
The identity
W_{1}^{d}=V^{d}
follows from the following reduced Cartesian square

\textstyle{W_{1}^{d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{red}}
\textstyle{S^{\times d-1}\times S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hilb}^{d,d-1}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sym}^{d-1}(S).}

Consider the following diagram

\textstyle{S^{\times d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{red}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\operatorname{Hilb}}^{d}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\textstyle{W^{d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{3}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sym}^{d}(S)}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\operatorname{Hilb}^{d}(S)}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\operatorname{Hilb}^{d,d-1}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ev}_{2}^{\mathrm{H}}}
\scriptstyle{(\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{\mathrm{H}},\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\mathrm{H}})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hilb}^{d-1}(S)\times S.}

By the base change, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{\vee}_{\operatorname{Hilb}}(\mathcal{Q}_{E}^{d})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{H}^{\vee}_{\operatorname{Hilb}}((\psi_{*}\eta^{*}E^{\boxtimes d})^{\mathrm{sign}}\otimes\det\mathcal{A}^{-1})

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{\mathrm{H}},\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\mathrm{H}})_{*}\mathrm{ev}_{2}^{\mathrm{H}*}((\psi_{*}\eta^{*}E^{\boxtimes d})^{\mathrm{sign}}\otimes\det\mathcal{A}^{-1})

\displaystyle\cong(\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{\mathrm{H}},\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\mathrm{H}})_{*}((\psi_{2*}\eta_{3}^{*}E^{\boxtimes d})^{\mathrm{sign}}\otimes\mathrm{ev}_{2}^{\mathrm{H}*}\det\mathcal{A}^{-1}).

Note that we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{ev}_{2}^{\mathrm{H}*}\det\mathcal{A}\cong(\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{\mathrm{H}},\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\mathrm{H}})^{*}(\det\mathcal{A}\boxtimes\mathcal{O}_{S})

since the line bundle on
S
with fiber
\det\chi(\mathcal{O}_{x})
for
x\in S
is a trivial
line bundle.
Therefore we have

(3.64)

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{\vee}_{\operatorname{Hilb}}(\mathcal{Q}_{E}^{d})\cong(\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{\mathrm{H}},\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\mathrm{H}})_{*}((\psi_{2*}\eta_{3}^{*}E^{\boxtimes d})^{\mathrm{sign}})\otimes\det\mathcal{A}^{-1}.

Consider the object
\eta_{3}^{*}E^{\boxtimes d}\in\mathrm{Perf}(W^{d})
. It is
\mathfrak{S}_{d}
-equivariantly
isomorphic to a totalization of the complex of the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{d}\eta_{3}^{*}E^{\boxtimes d}|_{W_{i}^{d}}\to\mathcal{R}_{2}\to\mathcal{R}_{3}\to\cdots

where each
\mathcal{R}_{k}
is supported on

(3.65)

\displaystyle\coprod_{1\leqslant i_{1}<\ldots<i_{k}\leqslant d}W_{i_{1}}^{d}\cap\cdots\cap W_{i_{k}}^{d}.

We have the decomposition

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{k}=\bigoplus_{1\leqslant i_{1}<\cdots<i_{k}\leqslant d}\mathcal{R}_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}}

where
\mathcal{R}_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}}
is supported on

W_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}}^{d}:=W_{i_{1}}^{d}\cap\cdots\cap W_{i_{k}}^{d}
.

For a fixed
1\leqslant i_{1}<\cdots<i_{k}\leqslant d
, let

\mathfrak{S}_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}}\subset\mathfrak{S}_{d}

be the subgroup which preserves the subset
\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\}\subset\{1,\ldots,d\}.

Then
\mathfrak{S}_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}}\cong\mathfrak{S}_{k}\times\mathfrak{S}_{d-k}
,
and the
\mathfrak{S}_{d}
-action on (
3.65
) restricted to

\mathfrak{S}_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}}
preserves
the component
W_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}}^{d}
.
Moreover its action on the subgroup
\mathfrak{S}_{k}\times\{1\}
is trivial
on
W_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}}^{d}
.
Therefore for
k\geqslant 2
, noting that
\mathrm{sign}_{\mathfrak{S}_{d}}|_{\mathfrak{S}_{k}\times\mathfrak{S}_{d-k}}=\mathrm{sign}_{\mathfrak{S}_{k}}\boxtimes\mathrm{sign}_{\mathfrak{S}_{d-k}}
, we have

\displaystyle(\psi_{2*}\mathcal{R}_{k})^{\mathrm{sign}}

\displaystyle\subset^{\oplus}(\psi_{2*}\mathcal{R}_{k})^{\text{sign for }\mathfrak{S}_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}}}

\displaystyle\subset^{\oplus}(\psi_{2*}\mathcal{R}_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}})^{\text{sign for }\mathfrak{S}_{k}\times\{1\}}\oplus(\text{other components})

\displaystyle=\{0\}\oplus(\text{other components}).

Here
A\subset^{\oplus}B
means that
A
is a direct summand of
B
.
Since the above holds for any
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}
,
it follows that

\displaystyle(\psi_{2*}\mathcal{R}_{k})^{\mathrm{sign}}=0,\ k\geqslant 2.

As for the contribution from
\mathcal{R}_{1}
, we use the following diagram

\textstyle{V^{d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{3}}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{4}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\operatorname{Hilb}}^{d-1}(S)\times S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S^{\times d-1}\times S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hilb}^{d,d-1}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{\mathrm{H}},\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\mathrm{H}})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hilb}^{d-1}(S)\times S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sym}^{d-1}(S)\times S.}

Then under the identification
W^{d}_{1}=V^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle(\eta_{3}^{*}E^{\boxtimes d})|_{W_{i}^{d}}=\sigma_{i}^{*}(\eta_{3}^{*}E^{\boxtimes d}|_{W_{1}^{d}})\cong\sigma_{i}^{*}\eta_{4}^{*}(E^{\boxtimes d-1}\boxtimes E).

It follows that

\displaystyle\left(\psi_{2*}\bigoplus_{i=1}^{d}(\eta_{3}^{*}E^{\boxtimes d})|_{W_{i}^{d}}\right)^{\mathrm{sign}}

\displaystyle=\left(\psi_{2*}\bigoplus_{i=1}^{d}\sigma_{i}^{*}\eta_{4}^{*}(E^{\boxtimes d-1}\boxtimes E)\right)^{\mathrm{sign}}

\displaystyle=\left(\psi_{3*}\eta_{4}^{*}(E^{\boxtimes d-1}\boxtimes E)\right)^{\text{sign for }\mathfrak{S}_{d-1}\times\{1\}}

\displaystyle\cong(\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{\mathrm{H}},\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\mathrm{H}})^{*}((\mathcal{Q}_{E}^{d-1}\otimes\det\mathcal{A})\boxtimes E).

Here the last isomorphism follows from the base change in the diagram (
3.5
).
By (
3.64
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}_{\operatorname{Hilb}}^{\vee}(\mathcal{Q}_{E}^{d})\cong(\mathcal{Q}_{E}^{d-1}\boxtimes E)\otimes(\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{\mathrm{H}},\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\mathrm{H}})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{Hilb}^{d,d-1}(S)}.

We have the isomorphism, for example see
[
43
, Equation (3.4)]

\displaystyle(\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{\mathrm{H}},\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\mathrm{H}})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{Hilb}^{d,d-1}(S)}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{Hilb}^{d-1}(S)\times S}.

Therefore the isomorphism (
3.55
) holds.
∎

3.6.
Proof of Theorem
3.3

Proof.

By Lemma
3.5
, it is enough to prove that (
3.5
) commutes.
Consider the composition

\displaystyle\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{W}^{\vee}}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}})\otimes\operatorname{Coh}(S)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Phi\otimes\operatorname{id}}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}})\otimes\operatorname{Coh}(S).

Its kernel object
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{W}}
relative over
\mathcal{B}
is given as follows.
For the diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}\times S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{13}}
\scriptstyle{p_{12}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}}\times S}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}}

We have

(3.70)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{W}}=p_{12}^{*}\mathcal{P}\otimes p_{13}^{*}\mathcal{E}\in\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}\times S).

The above object lies in
\operatorname{Coh}(-)
since
p_{12}
is flat and
\mathcal{E}
is a perfect
complex.

Suppose that
E\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{C}_{b})
is semistable,
so that the map
\tau_{E}
in (
3.39
) factors through

\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}}\subset\mathcal{H}
.
Then the pull-back of
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{W}}
by the map

(3.71)

\displaystyle\tau_{E}\times\operatorname{id}\colon\operatorname{Spec}k\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}\times S=\mathcal{H}_{b}^{\mathrm{greg}}\times S\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}\times S

is isomorphic to

(3.72)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{E}\boxtimes E,\ E\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(S).

Since it is a Cohen–Macaulay sheaf,
for any
k
-scheme
T
locally of finite type with a morphism
T\to\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}}
,
the pull-back of
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{W}}
to
T\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}\times S
is a

T
-flat family of Cohen–Macaulay sheaves, see
[
6
, Lemma 2.1]
.
Therefore
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{W}}\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}\times S)

and it is a Cohen–Macaulay sheaf flat over
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}}
.
Since
E
is supported on the corresponding spectral curve
\mathcal{C}_{b}
, we indeed
have

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{W}}\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{C})

which is a maximal Cohen–Macaulay sheaf, under the obvious fully-faithful functor

\displaystyle\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{C})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}\times S).

Similarly consider the composition

\displaystyle\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Phi}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{H}^{\vee}}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}})\otimes\operatorname{Coh}(S).

Its kernel object
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H}}
relative over
\mathcal{B}
is given as follows.
Consider the following diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\scriptstyle{q_{3}}
\scriptstyle{p_{3}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}ecke^{\mathrm{greg}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}\times S}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}}}

We have the induced morphism

(3.73)

\displaystyle(p_{3},q_{3})\colon\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}\times S

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H}}=(p_{3},q_{3})_{*}p_{2}^{*}\mathcal{P}\in\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}\times S).

The above object lies in
\operatorname{Coh}(-)
since the map (
3.73
) is proper (which follows from the properness of
q
) and
p_{2}
is
quasi-smooth.
By Proposition
3.7
, the pull-back of
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H}}
under
the map (
3.71
)
is also isomorphic to (
3.72
):

\displaystyle(\tau_{E}\times\operatorname{id})^{*}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H}}\cong\mathcal{P}_{E}\boxtimes E.

Therefore by the same argument for
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{W}}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H}}\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{C})

and is a maximal Cohen–Macaulay sheaf.

Note that
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{C}

has pure-dimension since
\mathcal{H}
has pure-dimension and

\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{B}
,
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{B}
are flat.
Therefore by the uniqueness of Cohen–Macaulay extension,
see
[
6
, Lemma 2.2]
,
it is enough to show that
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{W}}=\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H}}

outside a codimension two closed substack.
Note that the stack

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{C}

classifies tuples

(3.74)

\displaystyle(F_{1},F_{2},x),\ F_{i}\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{C}_{b}),\ b\in\mathcal{B},\ x\in\mathcal{C}_{b}

such that
F_{1}
is a semistable sheaf and
F_{2}
is generically a line bundle on
\mathcal{C}_{b}
.
We define the open substack

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}\subset\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{C}

to be consisting of (
3.74
) such that
F_{1}
is a line bundle on
\mathcal{C}_{b}

and
F_{2}
is a line bundle at
x
. It is clear that
the complement of
\mathcal{U}
is of codimension greater than or equal to two.
It is enough to show that

(3.75)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{W}}|_{\mathcal{U}}\cong\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H}}|_{\mathcal{U}}.

Over the open substack
\mathcal{U}
, the morphism (
3.73
) gives
an isomorphism

\displaystyle(p_{3},q_{3})\colon(p_{3},q_{3})^{-1}(\mathcal{U})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}\mathcal{U}.

Indeed the fiber of (
3.73
) at (
3.74
)
is given by
non-trivial exact sequences

(3.76)

\displaystyle 0\to F_{2}\to F_{2}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{O}_{x}\to 0.

Since
\mathcal{C}_{b}
is Gorenstein and
F_{2}
is a line bundle on
\mathcal{C}_{b}
at
x
,
we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{C}_{b}}^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{x},F_{2})=\operatorname{Hom}(F_{2},\mathcal{O}_{x}\otimes\omega_{\mathcal{C}_{b}})^{\vee}=k.

It follows that we have

\displaystyle(p_{3},q_{3})^{-1}(F_{1},F_{2},x)=\mathbb{G}_{m}/\mathbb{G}_{m}=\mathrm{pt}.

Therefore over
\mathcal{U}
, the object
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H}}|_{\mathcal{U}}
is a line
bundle whose fiber is

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{H}}|_{(F_{1},F_{2},x)}=\det\chi(F_{1}\otimes F_{2}^{\prime})\otimes\det\chi(F_{1})^{\vee}\otimes\det\chi(F_{2}^{\prime})^{\vee}\otimes\det\chi(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}_{b}}),

where
F_{2}^{\prime}
is the unique non-trivial extension (up to scalar)
in (
3.76
).
Using the exact sequence (
3.76
), the right-hand side is canonically identified with

\displaystyle\det\chi(F_{1}\otimes F_{2})\otimes\det\chi(F_{1})^{\vee}\otimes\det\chi(F_{2})^{\vee}\otimes\det\chi(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}_{b}})\otimes\det\chi(F_{1}|_{x}).

It is nothing but
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{W}}|_{(F_{1},F_{2},x)}
by the formula (
3.70
)
for
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{W}}
, therefore (
3.75
) holds.
∎

3.7.
A variant

We will also use the following variant of the diagram (
3.5
).
Let
\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}^{R}
be the functor

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}^{R}\colon\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}})\to\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}})\otimes\operatorname{Coh}(S)

defined by

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}^{R}(-)=p_{\mathcal{H}}^{!}(-)\otimes\mathbb{D}_{S\times\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{E})=p_{\mathcal{H}}^{*}(-)\otimes\mathcal{E}^{\vee}[1].

Here
\mathcal{E}^{\vee}=\mathcal{H}^{1}(\mathbb{D}_{S\times\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{E}))\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(S\times\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}})
, which is a
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}}
-flat family of pure one-dimensional sheaves on
S
.
Note that
p_{\mathcal{H}}
is not proper, but the above formula makes
sense since
\mathbb{D}_{S\times\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{E})
is supported on

\mathcal{C}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}}
which is proper over

\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}}
. The functor
\widetilde{W}^{R}
gives a right adjoint
of the functor
\mathrm{W}
in (
3.21
).

Similarly let
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}^{R}
be the functor

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}^{R}\colon\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}})\to\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}})\otimes\operatorname{Coh}(S)

defined by

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}^{R}(-)=(\mathrm{ev}_{2},\mathrm{ev}_{3})_{*}\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{!}(-).

Note that both
\mathrm{ev}_{1}
and
(\mathrm{ev}_{2},\mathrm{ev}_{3})
are quasi-smooth and proper by Lemma
3.1
.
It is a right adjoint functor of
\mathrm{H}
given by (
3.26
).
The proof of the following proposition is almost the same as the proof of the commutativity of (
3.5
). In Subsection
6.3
, we explain where to modify the proof.

Proposition 3.9
.

The following diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}^{R}}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}})\otimes\operatorname{Coh}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi\otimes\operatorname{id}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}^{R}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}})\otimes\operatorname{Coh}(S).}

4.
Dolbeault Langlands for reduced spectral curves

In this section, we prove Conjecture
2.10
over an open subset of
\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{red}}
,
with additional hypothesis; Whittaker normalization and some restriction of singularities of spectral curves.

4.1.
Higgs bundles with reduced spectral curves

Recall that
\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{red}}\subset\mathrm{B}
is the open subset corresponding
to reduced spectral curves.
We fix an open subset

(4.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}\subset\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{red,cl}}.

As in (
2.23
), we write

(4.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}=\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}\times_{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{B}\subset\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}^{\mathrm{cl}},

and
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{B}
denotes the universal curve over
\mathcal{B}

with projection

\pi\colon\mathcal{C}\to C\times\mathcal{B}
.
Note that
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}=\mathcal{H}
by the condition (
4.1
), and it is classical.

Let
\mathcal{N}\subset\Omega_{\mathcal{H}}[-1]

be the nilpotent cone, and

\displaystyle\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))_{w}\subset\operatorname{L}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))_{w}

the subcategory of nilpotent singular supports.
In this case, we have the following:

Lemma 4.1
.

We have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))=\mathrm{Perf}(\mathcal{H}(\chi)).

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))_{w}=\operatorname{L}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))_{w}\cap\mathrm{Perf}(\mathcal{H}(\chi)).

Proof.

For
b\in\mathcal{B}
, let
\mathcal{C}_{b}
be the reduced spectral curve with
irreducible components
C_{1},\ldots,C_{k}
.
Then any torsion-free object
E\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{C}_{b})

with rank one admits a filtration whose associated graded
is
\oplus_{i=1}^{k}L_{i}
for a line bundle
L_{i}
on
C_{i}
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Aut}(E)\subset\operatorname{Aut}(\oplus_{i=1}^{k}L_{i})=\mathbb{G}_{m}^{k}.

Since the Lie algebra of the torus does not have non-trivial
nilpotent elements, the nilpotent cone

\mathcal{N}\subset\Omega_{\mathcal{H}}[-1]

is the same as the zero section of
\Omega_{\mathcal{H}}[-1]\to\mathcal{H}
. Therefore the lemma holds from
[
5
, Theorem 4.2.6]
and noting that any compact
object of
\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))
is a perfect complex.
∎

4.2.
Whittaker normalization

We set

\displaystyle\chi_{0}:=\deg\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}_{b}}=(r^{2}-r)(1-g).

We have the following
Hitchin section

(4.3)

\displaystyle s\colon\mathrm{B}\to\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}(\chi_{0}).

It is induced by the structure sheaf of the universal spectral curve, i.e.
it sends
b\in\mathrm{B}
to the structure sheaf

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}_{b}}
.
Note that its image lies in
\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{reg}}
.
For the Arinkin sheaf
\mathrm{P}
in (
2.36
), we have the following:

Lemma 4.2
.

For the morphism

\displaystyle\operatorname{id}\times s\colon\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}(w)\to\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}(w)\times_{\mathrm{B}}\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{reg}},

we have
(\operatorname{id}\times s)^{*}\mathrm{P}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}(w)}
.

Proof.

The lemma immediately follows by the construction of Arinkin
sheaf
\mathcal{P}^{\sharp}
on
(\mathcal{H}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H})^{\sharp}
for
\mathcal{B}=\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{cl}}
, see Subsection
2.7
.
Indeed for the Hitchin section
s\colon\mathcal{B}\to\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{reg}}
,
and a rank one torsion-free sheaf
E\in\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{C}_{b})
, we have

\displaystyle(\operatorname{id}\times s)^{*}\mathcal{P}^{\sharp}|_{E}=\det\chi(E\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}_{b}})\otimes\det\chi(E)^{-1}\otimes\det\chi(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}_{b}})^{-1}\otimes\det\chi(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}_{b}})

which is canonically identified with
k
. Therefore

(\operatorname{id}\times s)^{*}\mathcal{P}^{\sharp}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}}
.
The lemma then follows by applying
\boxtimes\mathcal{O}_{k[-1]}
.
∎

The Hitchin section (
4.3
) is quasi-smooth, and it also factors through

\displaystyle s\colon\mathrm{B}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\overline{s}}}{{\to}}\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{ss}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle j}}{{\hookrightarrow}}\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}(\chi_{0}),

since
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}_{b}}\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{C}_{b})
is semistable (indeed stable).

Lemma 4.3
.

The functor

\displaystyle\overline{s}^{*}\colon\operatorname{L}(\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{ss}})_{w^{\prime}}\to\operatorname{Coh}(\mathrm{B})

admits a left adjoint

(4.4)

\displaystyle\overline{s}_{!}\colon\operatorname{Coh}(\mathrm{B})\to\operatorname{L}(\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{ss}})_{w^{\prime}}.

Proof.

Recall the diagram of good moduli spaces (
2.2
).
The composition

\displaystyle\mathrm{B}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\overline{s}}}{{\to}}\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{ss}}\to\mathrm{H}_{G}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{ss}}

is a closed immersion, which further factors through the quasi-smooth
closed immersion into the open subset
\mathrm{H}_{G}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{st}}\subset\mathrm{H}_{G}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{ss}}

\displaystyle\overline{s}^{\prime}\colon\mathrm{B}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{H}_{G}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{st}}.

Indeed it is a closed immersion between smooth varieties multiplied with
k[-1]
.

The left vertical arrow in (
2.2
) is a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-gerbe,
classified by a Brauer class
\beta
on
\mathrm{H}_{G}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{st}}
.
Since
\overline{s}^{{}^{\prime}*}\beta
is trivial on
\mathrm{B}
,
we have the functor

\displaystyle\overline{s}^{{}^{\prime}*}\colon\operatorname{Coh}(\mathrm{H}_{G}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{st}},\beta^{w^{\prime}})\to\operatorname{Coh}(\mathrm{B}).

Here the left-hand side is the category of
\beta^{w^{\prime}}
-twisted coherent sheaves on
\mathrm{H}_{G}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{st}}
.
Since
\overline{s}^{\prime}
is a closed immersion, it admits a left adjoint

(4.5)

\displaystyle\overline{s}^{{}^{\prime}}_{!}\colon\operatorname{Coh}(\mathrm{B})\to\operatorname{Coh}(\mathrm{H}_{G}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{st}},\beta^{w^{\prime}})\simeq\operatorname{Coh}(\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{st}})_{w^{\prime}}.

It is given by the formula

\displaystyle\overline{s}^{\prime}_{!}=\overline{s}^{\prime}_{*}[r^{2}(1-g)-1],

where the shift is determined by the relative dimension of
\overline{s}^{\prime}
.

Since
\overline{s}^{\prime}
is a closed immersion whose image is also closed in
\mathrm{H}_{G}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{ss}}
, the image of the
functor (
4.5
) naturally defines an object in the limit category of

\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{ss}}
.
Therefore the functor (
4.5
)
determines a functor (
4.4
), which is a left adjoint of
\overline{s}^{*}
by the construction.
∎

We have the pull-back functor

(4.6)

\displaystyle s^{*}\colon\operatorname{L}(\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}(\chi_{0}))_{w^{\prime}}\to\operatorname{Coh}(\mathrm{B}).

By Lemma
4.3
,
the functor (
4.6
) admits left adjoint

\displaystyle s_{!}\colon\operatorname{Coh}(\mathrm{B})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\overline{s}_{!}}}{{\to}}\operatorname{L}(\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{ss}})_{w^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle j_{!}}}{{\hookrightarrow}}\operatorname{L}(\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}(\chi_{0}))_{w^{\prime}}

where
j_{!}
is the left adjoint of
j^{*}
, see Theorem
2.14
.

Since the above constructions are linear over
\mathrm{B}
,
the same construction and the proof of Theorem
2.14
in
[
34
, Theorem 7.19]
also apply after
the base change
\mathcal{B}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{B}
given by (
4.2
).
It gives the functor

(4.7)

\displaystyle s_{!}\colon\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{B})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\overline{s}_{!}}}{{\to}}\operatorname{L}(\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{ss}})_{w^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle j_{!}}}{{\hookrightarrow}}\operatorname{L}(\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0}))_{w^{\prime}}.

The image of the above functor lies in the subcategory of
nilpotent singular supports

\displaystyle s_{!}\colon\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{B})\to\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0}))_{w^{\prime}}

since the image of
\overline{s}
lies in the smooth locus of
\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0})
and
j_{!}
preserves nilpotent singular support condition, see
[
34
, Section 8.10]
. It gives a left adjoint of the
functor

\displaystyle s^{*}\colon\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0}))_{w^{\prime}}\to\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{B}).

We conjecture the following property of the functor
\Phi
in (
2.31
), which corresponds to Whittaker normalization.

Conjecture 4.4
.

There is an isomorphism

\displaystyle\Phi(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}})\cong s_{!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}}\in\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0}))_{w^{\prime}}.

Remark 4.5
.

The object
s_{!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}}
is more exotic than

\overline{s}_{!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}}
. Indeed in the situation of Example
2.3
,
the support of
\overline{s}_{!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}}
on
h^{-1}(b)
is just one point
corresponding to
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}_{b}}
in
\mathcal{P}^{0,0}
. However after applying
j_{!}
,
the object
s_{!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}}=j_{!}\overline{s}_{!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}}
is supported
beyond
\mathcal{P}^{0,0}
; indeed for
l\geqslant 2
and
1\leqslant m\leqslant l-1
, let
p_{l,m}
be the point

\displaystyle p_{l,m}

\displaystyle={(\mathcal{O}_{C_{1}}((l-m)x_{1}+mx_{2}),\mathcal{O}_{C_{2}}(-mx_{1}+(m-l)x_{2})})

\displaystyle\in\mathrm{Pic}^{l}(C_{1})\times\mathrm{Pic}^{-l}(C_{2}).

Then one can calculate that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Supp}(s_{!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}})\cap\mathcal{P}^{l,-l}=\{p_{l,m}:1\leqslant m\leqslant l-1\}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mathbb{G}_{m},

in particular
s_{!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}}
spreads beyond the semistable locus.
The details of this computation may appear elsewhere.

4.3.
Wilson/Hecke compatibility under Whittaker normalization

In the rest of this section, under Conjecture
4.4
, we show that the functor
(
2.31
) restricts to the functor

\displaystyle\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}}\to\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))_{w^{\prime}}

which is fully faithful, and indeed an equivalence under an additional hypothesis in Assumption
4.18
. We will prove it using the compatibility of Wilson/Hecke operators in the previous section.

For
d\geqslant 0
, we consider the functor induced by the Wilson operators
applied to
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}}\in\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{0}

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{d}\colon\operatorname{Coh}(S^{\times d})\to\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{d}.

We also consider the functor induced by Hecke operator applied to

s_{!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}}\in\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0}))_{w^{\prime}}
,

(4.8)

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}_{d}\colon\operatorname{Coh}(S^{\times d})\to\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0}-d))_{w^{\prime}}.

We have the following corollary of the result in the
previous section:

Corollary 4.6
.

Under Conjecture
4.4
, the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\operatorname{Coh}(S^{\times d})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{W}_{d}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Coh}(S^{\times d})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{H}_{d}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0}-d))_{w^{\prime}}.}

Proof.

The corollary follows by applying Theorem
3.3

d
-times.
∎

The image of the functor
\mathrm{H}_{d}
lies in the limit category by the following lemma:

Lemma 4.7
.

The image of the functor (
4.8
) lies in

\displaystyle\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0}-d))_{w^{\prime}}\subset\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0}-d))_{w^{\prime}}.

Proof.

As in Lemma
3.2
,
the Hecke operator in (
3.26
)

(4.13)

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}\colon\operatorname{Coh}(S)\otimes\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H})\to\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H})

restricts to the functor of limit categories

(4.14)

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}\colon\operatorname{Coh}(S)\otimes\operatorname{L}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))_{w^{\prime}}\to\operatorname{L}(\mathcal{H}(\chi-1))_{w^{\prime}}.

Indeed as the Hecke operator is linear over
\mathcal{B}
, the argument of Lemma
3.2
goes through by replacing
\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}
with preimages of
open subsets of
\mathrm{B}
. Moreover the trivial derived structure
k[-1]

does not affect the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-weights in the definition of limit categories, therefore the functor
\mathrm{H}
restricts to the functor (
4.14
).

Since
\mathrm{ev}_{1}
in the diagram (
3.3
) is quasi-smooth and proper by
Lemma
3.1
, the push-forward
\mathrm{ev}_{1*}
preserves perfect complexes, see
[
41
, Theorem 0.3]
. Therefore
the functor (
4.13
) also restricts to the functor

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}\colon\operatorname{Coh}(S)\otimes\mathrm{Perf}(\mathcal{H})\to\mathrm{Perf}(\mathcal{H}).

It follows that, by Lemma
4.1
, the functor (
4.14
) further restricts to the functor

(4.15)

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}\colon\operatorname{Coh}(S)\otimes\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))_{w^{\prime}}\to\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi-1))_{w^{\prime}}.

Iterating this
d
-times, we obtain the lemma.
∎

Together with the compact generation under Wilson operators proved in the next subsection, the Arinkin sheaf determines, assuming Conjecture
4.4
, a functor to the limit category that also satisfies Wilson/Hecke compatibility:

Corollary 4.8
.

Under Conjecture
4.4
, the
functor
\Phi
restricts to the functor

\displaystyle\Phi\colon\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}}\to\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))_{w^{\prime}},

and we have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\operatorname{Coh}(S^{\times d})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{W}_{d}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Coh}(S^{\times d})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{H}_{d}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0}-d))_{w^{\prime}}.}

Proof.

In Corollary
4.14
, we show that
\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})

is compactly generated by the images of Wilson operators applied to
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}}
, hence

\mathrm{Perf}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})
is split generated by the images of
these objects. Then
the corollary follows by combining Corollary
4.6

and Lemma
4.7
.
∎

We next consider the compatibility with the right adjoints of Wilson/Hecke operators:

Proposition 4.9
.

Under Conjecture
4.4
, there is a commutative
diagram

\textstyle{\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{W}_{d}^{R}}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{\operatorname{QCoh}(S^{\times d})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0}-d))_{w^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{H}_{d}^{R}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{QCoh}(S^{\times d}).}

Here
\mathrm{W}_{d}^{R}
,
\mathrm{H}_{d}^{R}
are right adjoint functors
of
\mathrm{W}_{d}
,
\mathrm{H}_{d}
in the diagram (
4.8
).

Proof.

By Proposition
3.9
and noting
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}=\mathcal{H}
,
the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}^{R}}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}})\otimes\operatorname{Coh}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi\otimes\operatorname{id}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}^{R}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H})\otimes\operatorname{Coh}(S).}

Similarly to (
4.15
), the bottom functor restricts to the functor

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}^{R}\colon\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))_{w^{\prime}}\to\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi+1))_{w^{\prime}}\otimes\operatorname{Coh}(S).

Then by Corollary
4.8
, the diagram (
4.3
) restricts
to the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}^{R}}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}-1}\otimes\operatorname{Coh}(S)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi\otimes\operatorname{id}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))_{w^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}^{R}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi+1))_{w^{\prime}}\otimes\operatorname{Coh}(S).}

By applying it
d
-times, we have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{d}^{R}}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}-d}\otimes\operatorname{Coh}(S^{\times d})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi\otimes\operatorname{id}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))_{w^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{d}^{R}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi+d))_{w^{\prime}}\otimes\operatorname{Coh}(S^{\times d}).}

We also have the commutative diagram by Lemma
4.2

\textstyle{\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{B})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0}))_{w^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s^{*}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{B}).}

The bottom horizontal arrow is isomorphic to

\displaystyle s^{*}(-)\cong\operatorname{Hom}(s_{!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}},-)\colon\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0}))_{w^{\prime}}\to\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{B})

since
s_{!}
is left adjoint to

, see Lemma
4.3
.
It is straightforward to check that the

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{d}^{R}\colon\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{d}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathrm{W}}_{d}^{R}}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{0}\otimes\operatorname{Coh}(S^{\times d})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Gamma(h_{*}(-))\otimes\operatorname{id}}}{{\to}}\operatorname{QCoh}(S^{\times d}),

is a right adjoint functor of
\mathrm{W}_{d}
. Similarly the
composition

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}_{d}^{R}\colon\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0}-d))_{w^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{d}^{R}}}{{\to}}\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0}))_{w^{\prime}}\otimes\operatorname{Coh}(S^{\times d})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\operatorname{Hom}(s_{!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}},-)\otimes\operatorname{id}}}{{\to}}\operatorname{QCoh}(S^{\times d})

is a right adjoint functor of
\mathrm{H}_{d}
.
Therefore the proposition follows.
∎

Fix
c\in C
. Then for
\chi\in\mathbb{Z}
and

d>0
such that
r|(d+\chi)
, there is an equivalence,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{d},

given as follows, see Remark
2.12

\displaystyle(-)\mapsto(-)\otimes\det\mathcal{F}_{c}^{\otimes(\chi^{\prime}+d)/r}.

By composing with
\mathrm{W}_{d}
, we obtain the functor

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{d,c}\colon\operatorname{Coh}(S^{\times d})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{W}_{d}}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{d}\simeq\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}}

with right adjoint

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{d,c}^{R}\colon\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}}\simeq\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{d}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{W}_{d}^{R}}}{{\to}}\operatorname{QCoh}(S^{\times d}).

There is also an equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))_{w^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0}-d))_{w^{\prime}}

induced by the isomorphism, see Remark
2.12

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(\chi)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0}-d),\ (-)\mapsto(-)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}((\chi_{0}-d-\chi)/r\cdot c).

By composing with
\mathrm{H}_{d}
, we obtain the functor

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}_{d,c}\colon\operatorname{Coh}(S^{\times d})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{H}_{d}}}{{\to}}\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0}-d))_{w^{\prime}}\simeq\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))_{w^{\prime}}

with right adjoint

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}_{d,c}^{R}\colon\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))_{w^{\prime}}\simeq\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0}-d))_{w^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{H}_{d}^{R}}}{{\to}}\operatorname{QCoh}(S^{\times d}).

As a corollary of the above arguments, we have the following:

Corollary 4.10
.

Under Conjecture
4.4
, for
d>0
with

r|(d+\chi)
and
c\in C
, we have the commutative diagrams

\textstyle{\operatorname{Coh}(S^{\times d})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{W}_{d,c}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Coh}(S^{\times d})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{H}_{d,c}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))_{w^{\prime}}}

\textstyle{\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{W}_{d,c}^{R}}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\operatorname{QCoh}(S^{\times d})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))_{w^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{H}_{d,c}^{R}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{QCoh}(S^{\times d}).}

Let

\displaystyle\Phi\colon\operatorname{IndCoh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}}\to\operatorname{IndL}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))_{w^{\prime}}

be the functor defined by taking the ind-completion of the functor in
Corollary
4.8
, and using the compact generations of both sides, see Theorem
2.8
, Remark
2.11
.
Since it preserves the compact objects,
by the adjoint functor theorem it admits a continuous
right adjoint

\displaystyle\Phi^{R}\colon\operatorname{IndL}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))_{w^{\prime}}\to\operatorname{IndCoh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}}.

Corollary 4.11
.

Under Conjecture
4.4
, for
d>0
with

r|(d+\chi)
and
c\in C
, we have the commutative diagrams,
where each functor is continuous

\textstyle{\operatorname{IndCoh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{W}_{d,c}^{R}}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\operatorname{QCoh}(S^{\times d})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{IndL}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))_{w^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{H}_{d,c}^{R}}
\scriptstyle{\Phi^{R}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{QCoh}(S^{\times d}).}

Proof.

The commutativity of
\Phi
with
\mathrm{W}_{d,c}^{R},\mathrm{H}_{d,c}^{R}
follows by taking the ind-completions
of the right diagram in Corollary
4.10
.
The commutativity of
\Phi^{R}
with
\mathrm{W}_{d,c}^{R},\mathrm{H}_{d,c}^{R}
follows by taking the right adjoints
of the ind-completions of the left diagram in Corollary
4.10
.
∎

4.4.
Compact generation by Wilson operators

Here we show the compact generation of
\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})
under Wilson operators.

For
A\in\operatorname{Coh}(S)
, let

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{A}\colon\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{*}\to\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{*+1}

be the corresponding Wilson operator.
It is given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{A}(-)=(-)\otimes p_{\mathcal{H}*}(p_{S}^{*}A\otimes\mathcal{E})

where
p_{S},p_{\mathcal{H}}
are the projections from
S\times\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}
.
By the construction, it preserves
perfect complexes

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{A}\colon\mathrm{Perf}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{*}\to\mathrm{Perf}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{*+1}.

We fix
c\in C
and consider the line bundle

\displaystyle L_{c}:=\det(\mathcal{F}_{c})\in\mathrm{Pic}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}).

It has
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-weight
r
, so induces the equivalence

\displaystyle\otimes L_{c}\colon\mathrm{Perf}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{*}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{Perf}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{*+r}.

We have the following proposition:

Proposition 4.12
.

The objects of the form

(4.44)

\displaystyle\otimes L_{c}^{-m}\circ\mathrm{W}_{A_{d}}\circ\cdots\circ\mathrm{W}_{A_{1}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}})\in\mathrm{Perf}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})

for
m\geqslant 0
and
A_{1},\ldots,A_{d}\in\operatorname{Coh}(S)

compactly generate
\mathrm{QCoh}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})
.

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}\subset\mathrm{Perf}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})

be the subcategory split generated by objects of the form (
4.44
).
Since
\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}
is QCA, an object in
\mathbf{W}
is a compact object in
\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})
by
[
13
, Corollary 1.4.3]
.
Therefore it is enough to show that, if
M\in\mathrm{QCoh}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})

satisfies

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbf{W},M)=0,

then
M=0
.

Let
T\in\operatorname{Coh}(C)
be a locally free sheaf such that

\displaystyle(\operatorname{rank}(T),\deg(T))=(r,-w+r(g-1)).

Then for any Higgs bundle
(F,\theta)
with
\operatorname{rank}(F)=r
and
\deg(F)=w
,
we have

\chi(T\otimes F)=0
. Let
\pi\colon S\to C
be the projection. Then the line bundle

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{T}:=\det p_{\mathcal{H}*}(p_{C}^{*}T\otimes\mathcal{F})=\det p_{\mathcal{H}*}(p_{S}^{*}\pi^{*}T\otimes\mathcal{E})\in\mathrm{Pic}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})

has
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-weight zero, and it descends to an ample line bundle on the
good moduli space, see
[
25
, Section 8.4]

(4.45)

\displaystyle q_{\mathcal{H}}\colon\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}\to\mathrm{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}.

Namely we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{T}=q_{\mathcal{H}}^{*}\mathrm{L}_{T},\ \mathrm{L}_{T}\in\mathrm{Pic}(\mathrm{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})

for an ample line bundle
\mathrm{L}_{T}
. By the ampleness of
\mathrm{L}_{T}
, there is
\ell\geqslant 0

such that its powers

(4.46)

\displaystyle\{\mathrm{L}_{T}^{\otimes k}\}_{0\leqslant k\leqslant\ell}\subset\mathrm{Perf}(\mathrm{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})

compactly generate
\mathrm{QCoh}(\mathrm{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})
, see
[
9
, Lemma 4.2.2, 4.2.4]
.

For
m\gg 0
, the object

\displaystyle p_{\mathcal{H}*}(p_{C}^{*}(T\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(mc))\otimes\mathcal{F})\in\mathrm{Perf}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})

is a vector bundle of rank
mr^{2}
, and

\displaystyle\det p_{\mathcal{H}*}(p_{C}^{*}(T\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(mc))\otimes\mathcal{F})\hookrightarrow(p_{\mathcal{H}*}(p_{C}^{*}(T\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(mc))\otimes\mathcal{F}))^{\otimes mr^{2}}

is a direct summand. Moreover we have

\displaystyle\det p_{\mathcal{H}*}(p_{C}^{*}(T\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(mc))\otimes\mathcal{F})=\mathcal{L}_{T}\otimes L_{c}^{\otimes mr}.

It follows that we have the direct summand

(4.47)

\displaystyle(-)\otimes\mathcal{L}_{T}\subset^{\oplus}\overbrace{\mathrm{W}_{\pi^{*}(T\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(mc))}\circ\cdots\circ\mathrm{W}_{\pi^{*}(T\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(mc))}}^{mr^{2}}(-)\otimes L_{c}^{-mr}.

For
W\in\mathbf{W}
, we have
W\otimes\mathcal{L}_{T}^{\otimes k}\in\mathbf{W}

for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}
by (
4.47
). Therefore
\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbf{W},M)=0
implies that

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}(W\otimes q_{\mathcal{H}}^{*}\mathrm{L}_{T}^{\otimes k},M)=\Gamma(q_{\mathcal{H}*}(W^{\vee}\otimes M)\otimes\mathrm{L}_{T}^{\otimes-k})=0

for any
k
. By the compact generation by the objects (
4.46
), we conclude that

(4.48)

\displaystyle q_{\mathcal{H}*}(W^{\vee}\otimes M)=0

for any
W\in\mathbf{W}
.

Now each point
y\in\mathrm{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}

corresponds to a polystable Higgs bundle

\displaystyle E_{y}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{a}V_{i}\otimes(F_{i},\theta_{i})

where
\deg F_{i}/\operatorname{rank}(F_{i})=w/r
and
E_{i}=(F_{i},\theta_{i})
is stable.
We have the moment map

\displaystyle\mu\colon\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(E_{y},E_{y})=\bigoplus_{i,j}\operatorname{Hom}(V_{i},V_{j})\otimes\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(E_{i},E_{j})\to\operatorname{Ext}^{2}(E_{y},E_{y})=\mathfrak{g}(v)

given by
x\mapsto x\cup x
,
where
\mathfrak{g}(v)
is the Lie algebra of

G(v)=\prod_{i=1}^{a}\operatorname{GL}(V_{i})
.
Then formally locally at
y
,
the good moduli space map (
4.45
) is
isomorphic to the following quotient stack, see
[
33
, Remark 4.2]

\displaystyle\mu^{-1}(0)/G(v)\to\mu^{-1}(0)/\!\!/G(v).

Let
f_{c}:=\mathcal{O}_{\pi^{-1}(c)}\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(S)
.
The object

\displaystyle p_{\mathcal{H}*}(\mathcal{F}_{c})=\mathrm{W}_{f_{c}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}})\in\mathbf{W}\subset\mathrm{Perf}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})

is, on the above formal local model, isomorphic to

(4.49)

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i=1}^{a}V_{i}^{\oplus\operatorname{rank}(F_{i})}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mu^{-1}(0)/G(v)}\subset\mathrm{Perf}(\mu^{-1}(0)/G(v)).

Since the objects
V_{i}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mu^{-1}(0)/G(v)}
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant a

split generate
\mathrm{Perf}(\mu^{-1}(0)/G(v))
,
the object (
4.49
) gives a compact generator of
\mathrm{QCoh}(\mu^{-1}(0)/G(v))
.
Therefore the vanishing (
4.48
) implies that

M=0
formally locally at
y
. Since it holds for any
y
, we have
M=0
.
∎

Remark 4.13
.

Alternatively, for
A\in\operatorname{Coh}(S)
, consider

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{A}^{R}(-)=(-)\otimes p_{\mathcal{H}*}(p_{S*}(A)\otimes\mathcal{E})^{\vee}.

Then
Proposition
4.12
implies that the objects of the form

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{f_{c}}^{R}\circ\cdots\circ\mathrm{W}_{f_{c}}^{R}\circ\mathrm{W}_{A_{d}}\circ\cdots\circ\mathrm{W}_{A_{1}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}})

compactly generates
\mathrm{QCoh}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})
.

From the proof of the above proposition, we can take
d
to
be bounded above. Therefore we have the following corollary:

Corollary 4.14
.

For
d\gg 0
, the image of the functor

\displaystyle\operatorname{Coh}(S^{\times d})\to\mathrm{Perf}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{d}

given by

\displaystyle A_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes A_{d}\mapsto\mathrm{W}_{A_{d}}\circ\cdots\circ\mathrm{W}_{A_{1}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}})

compactly generates
\mathrm{QCoh}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{d}
.

Remark 4.15
.

The proof of Proposition
4.12
and Corollary
4.14
work without the assumption

\mathcal{B}\subset\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{red,cl}}
. Indeed the same result holds for
\mathcal{B}=\mathrm{B}
.

As a corollary of the compact generation by Wilson operators,
we have the conservativity of the right adjoint functors
of Wilson operators in Corollary
4.11
for
d\gg 0
.

Corollary 4.16
.

For
A\in\operatorname{IndCoh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}}
,
suppose that
\mathrm{W}_{d,c}^{R}(A)=0
for
d\gg 0

with
r|(d+\chi)
and any
c\in C
.
Then
A=0
.

Proof.

This corollary is a direct consequence of Corollary
4.14
and Lemma
4.17
,
noting the following from Lemma
4.1

under the assumption
\mathcal{B}\subset\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{red,cl}}

\displaystyle\operatorname{IndCoh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}}=\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}}.

∎

We have used the following obvious lemma:

Lemma 4.17
.

Let
\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathcal{C}_{2}
be compactly generated dg-categories
and
F\colon\mathcal{C}_{1}\to\mathcal{C}_{2}
be a continuous functor which
preserves compact objects
\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\mathrm{cp}}
. Let
F^{R}\colon\mathcal{C}_{2}\to\mathcal{C}_{1}
be the continuous right adjoint of
F
.
Then
\mathcal{C}_{2}
is compactly generated by
F(\mathcal{C}_{1}^{\mathrm{cp}})
if and only if
F^{R}
is conservative.

Proof.

The lemma follows that, for
A\in\mathcal{C}_{2}
, we have

\operatorname{Hom}(F(\mathcal{C}_{1}^{\mathrm{cp}}),A)=0
if and only if

\operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal{C}_{1}^{\mathrm{cp}},F^{R}(A))=0
, if and only if

F^{R}(A)=0
by the compact generation of
\mathcal{C}_{1}
.
∎

4.5.
Compact generation by Hecke operators

We prove similar statements as in the previous subsection for the Hecke operators. However we need to impose the following condition for the open subset (
4.1
):

Assumption 4.18
.

For
b\in\mathcal{B}
,
let
x\in\mathcal{C}_{b}
be a singular point
and
I_{x}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{C}_{b},x}
the conductor ideal.
Then we assume that
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{C}_{b},x}/I_{x}
is a chain ring for any
b
and
x
, i.e.
it is of the form
k[t]/t^{m}
for some
m\geqslant 1
.

Example 4.19
.

(1) If
G=\operatorname{GL}_{2}
, then
\mathcal{B}=\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{red,cl}}
satisfies the above condition.
Indeed in this case, since
\mathcal{C}_{b}\to C
is a double cover, the ring
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{C}_{b},x}

is of the form
k[[x,y]]/(y^{2}-x^{m})
for some
m\geqslant 1
.
In particular,

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{C}_{b},x}/I_{x}
is isomorphic to
k[t]/(t^{m/2})
when
m
is even,
and
k[t]/(t^{(m-1)/2})
when
m
is odd, as a ring.

(2) If
\mathcal{C}_{b}
has at worst nodal singularities, then
it satisfies the condition in Assumption
4.18
.

Remark 4.20
.

The condition in Assumption
4.18
is equivalent to
that
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathcal{C}_{b},x}/I_{x}
has only a finite number of
ideals. This property will be used in the proof of Lemma
4.22
.

Contrary to the case of Wilson operators, we first prove that the right adjoints of Hecke operators are conservative under Assumption
4.18
:

Proposition 4.21
.

For
A\in\operatorname{IndL}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))_{w^{\prime}}
,
suppose that
\mathrm{H}_{d,c}^{R}(A)=0
for
d\gg 0

with
r|(d+\chi)
and any
c\in C
. Then,
under Assumption
4.18
,
A=0
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{B}
be the universal spectral curve over

\mathcal{B}
.
By the construction, the functor
\mathrm{H}_{d,c}^{R}
factors through

(4.50)

\displaystyle\operatorname{QCoh}((\mathcal{C})^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i_{*}}}{{\to}}\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathcal{B}\times S^{\times d})\to\operatorname{QCoh}(S^{\times d})

where the first functor is the push-forward along the closed immersion

\displaystyle i\colon(\mathcal{C})^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{B}\times S^{\times d}

and the second functor is the push-forward along the second projection.
Both functors in (
4.50
) are conservative; the first functor is conservative as
i
is a closed immersion and the second one is conservative as
\mathcal{B}
is quasi-affine.
Therefore its composition is also conservative.
Then it is enough to show the claim by replacing
\mathrm{H}_{d,c}^{R}
with

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{H}}_{d,c}^{R}\colon\operatorname{IndL}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))_{w^{\prime}}\to\operatorname{QCoh}((\mathcal{C})^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}).

We have the following diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}ecke\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{((\mathrm{ev}_{3})^{d},\mathrm{ev}_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ev}_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0}-d)\simeq\mathcal{H}(\chi)}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{C})^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}\times s}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{C})^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0}).}

The stack
\mathcal{U}
over
b\in\mathcal{B}
consists of filtrations in
\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{C}_{b})

(4.51)

\displaystyle F_{0}\subset F_{1}\subset\cdots\subset F_{d}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}_{b}}

such that
F_{i}/F_{i-1}\cong\mathcal{O}_{x_{i}}
for
x_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{b}
, and the
map
p
sends (
4.51
) to
F_{0}
.
By the construction of
\mathrm{H}_{d,c}^{R}
,
we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{H}}_{d,c}^{R}(-)=(\operatorname{id}\times s)^{*}((\mathrm{ev}_{3})^{d},\mathrm{ev}_{2})_{*}(\mathrm{ev}_{1})^{!}(-).

By the base change, it is enough to show that for
A\in\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))
satisfying

q_{*}p^{*}A=0
for
d\gg 0
, we have
A=0
.
Here we have used the fact that
(\mathrm{ev}_{1})^{!}
and
(\mathrm{ev}_{1})^{*}
differ by
taking the tensor line bundle (up to shift) pulled back via
((\mathrm{ev}_{3})^{d},\mathrm{ev}_{2})
.

We define

(4.52)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\leqslant k}\subset\mathcal{H}(\chi)

to be the substack whose fiber at
b\in\mathcal{B}
consists of

E\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{C}_{b})
which admits an exact sequence

(4.53)

\displaystyle 0\to E\to\mathcal{L}\to Q\to 0

where
\mathcal{L}
is a line bundle on
\mathcal{C}_{b}
and
Q
is a zero-dimensional
sheaf on
\mathcal{C}_{b}
with
\chi(Q)\leqslant k
; for example see Remark
4.27
when
\mathcal{C}_{b}
is nodal.
Note that
\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\leqslant 0}
is the regular locus of
\mathcal{H}(\chi)
.
One can easily check that (
4.52
) is an open substack,
and
\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\leqslant k}=\mathcal{H}(\chi)
for
k\gg 0
.
We have the open immersions

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\leqslant 0}\subset\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\leqslant 1}\subset\cdots\subset\mathcal{H}(\chi).

We define the following closed substack
with reduced structure

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\geqslant k}:=\mathcal{H}(\chi)\setminus\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\leqslant k-1}\subset\mathcal{H}(\chi).

Then we have the sequence of closed substacks

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(\chi)\hookleftarrow\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\geqslant 1}\hookleftarrow\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\geqslant 2}\hookleftarrow\cdots

We set

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{k}:=\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\geqslant k}\setminus\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\geqslant k+1}\subset\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\geqslant k}

which is an open substack of
\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\geqslant k}
.

Let
p_{\geqslant k}
be the induced map from
p

(4.54)

\displaystyle p_{\geqslant k}\colon\mathcal{U}_{\geqslant k}:=(p^{-1}(\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\geqslant k}))^{\mathrm{red}}\to\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\geqslant k}.

Then the restriction of
q
to

\mathcal{U}_{\geqslant k}

factors through

\displaystyle q_{\geqslant k}\colon\mathcal{U}_{\geqslant k}\to(\mathcal{C})^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}_{\geqslant k}\subset(\mathcal{C})^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}

where

(\mathcal{C})^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}_{\geqslant k}
is the
reduced closed subscheme consisting of points

(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})
on
\mathcal{C}_{b}
such that the number of
1\leqslant i\leqslant d
with

x_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{b}^{\mathrm{sg}}
is bigger than or equal to
k
, and

\mathcal{C}_{b}^{\mathrm{sg}}\subset\mathcal{C}_{b}
is the set of singular
points.
We set

\displaystyle(\mathcal{C})^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}_{k}:=(\mathcal{C})^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}_{\geqslant k}\setminus(\mathcal{C})^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}_{\geqslant k+1},

and define the following open substack of
\mathcal{U}_{\geqslant k}

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{k}:=q_{\geqslant k}^{-1}((\mathcal{C})^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}_{k})\subset\mathcal{U}_{\geqslant k}.

Then the map (
4.54
) restricts to the map

\displaystyle p_{k}\colon\mathcal{U}_{k}\to\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{k}.

Indeed for a filtration (
4.51
), suppose that there is
I\subset\{1,\ldots,d\}
with
\sharp I=k
and
x_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{b}^{\mathrm{sg}}

if and only if
i\in I
.
Then we have the exact sequence on
\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{C}_{b})

(4.55)

\displaystyle 0\to F_{0}\to\mathcal{L}\to Q\to 0

where
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}_{b}}(-\sum_{i\notin I}x_{i})
is a line bundle
on
\mathcal{C}_{b}
and
Q
is supported on
x_{i}
for
i\in I
. This implies that

F_{0}
lies in
\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{k}
.
The above construction is summarized in the following diagram

\textstyle{(\mathcal{C})^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{U}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q_{k}}
\scriptstyle{p_{k}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{C})^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}_{\geqslant k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{U}_{\geqslant k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q_{\geqslant k}}
\scriptstyle{p_{\geqslant k}}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{red}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\geqslant k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{C})^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}(\chi).}

We also define

\displaystyle(\mathcal{C})^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}_{\leqslant k}:=(\mathcal{C})^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}\setminus(\mathcal{C})^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}_{\geqslant k+1}

which is an open subset of
(\mathcal{C})^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}
.
We define the open substack
\mathcal{U}_{\circ}\subset\mathcal{U}
by the Cartesian square

\textstyle{\mathcal{U}_{\circ}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q|_{\mathcal{U}_{\circ}}}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\textstyle{\mathcal{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{C})^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}_{\leqslant k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{C})^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}.}

Note that

(4.62)

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{k}=\mathcal{U}_{\geqslant k}\cap\mathcal{U}_{\circ}

and
\mathcal{U}_{k}
is an open substack of
\mathcal{U}_{\geqslant k}
and is a closed substack of
\mathcal{U}_{\circ}
.

Now by induction on
k
, suppose that

\displaystyle A\in\operatorname{QCoh}_{\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\geqslant k}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))

and it satisfies
q_{*}p^{*}A=0
.
Then we have

\displaystyle(p^{*}A)|_{\mathcal{U}_{\circ}}\in\operatorname{QCoh}_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}(\mathcal{U}_{\circ}),\ (q|_{\mathcal{U}_{\circ}})_{*}((p^{*}A)|_{\mathcal{U}_{\circ}})=0.

By Lemma
4.22
below, it follows that
(p^{*}A)|_{\mathcal{U}_{\circ}}=0
.
Take a quasi-compact open substack
\mathcal{H}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}}\subset\mathcal{H}(\chi)

and below we use the notation in Lemma
4.23
.
Then

\displaystyle(p^{*}A)|_{\mathcal{U}_{\circ}^{\mathrm{qc}}}=(p|_{\mathcal{U}_{\circ}^{\mathrm{qc}}})^{*}(A|_{\mathcal{H}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}}})=0.

By Lemma
4.24
, for
d\gg 0
, we conclude that

\displaystyle A|_{\mathcal{H}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}}}\in\operatorname{QCoh}_{\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\geqslant k+1}^{\mathrm{qc}}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}}).

Since the above holds for any quasi-compact

\mathcal{H}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}}\subset\mathcal{H}(\chi)
, we conclude that

A
is supported on
\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\geqslant k+1}
. By the induction on
k
,
we have
A=0
.
∎

We have used the following lemmas:

Lemma 4.22
.

The map
q_{k}
in the diagram (
4.5
) is finite. In particular, the functor

\displaystyle(q|_{\mathcal{U}_{\circ}})_{*}\colon\operatorname{QCoh}_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}(\mathcal{U}_{\circ})\to\operatorname{QCoh}((\mathcal{C})^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}_{\leqslant k})

is conservative.

Proof.

Since
q_{k}
is proper it is enough to show that
q_{k}
is quasi-finite.
For a point

(x_{i})_{1\leqslant i\leqslant d}\in(\mathcal{C}_{b})^{d}
,
suppose that there is a subset
I\subset\{1,\ldots,d\}
with
\sharp I=k
such that

x_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{b}^{\mathrm{sg}}
if and only if
i\in I
.
Let a filtration (
4.51
)
corresponds to a fiber at
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})
in
\mathcal{U}_{k}
.
Then we have the exact sequence on
\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{C}_{b})

\displaystyle 0\to F_{0}\to\mathcal{L}\to Q\to 0

as in (
4.55
).
Then by the definition of
\mathcal{U}_{k}
, we have that

\displaystyle k=\min\{\delta:\mbox{there is }0\to F_{0}\to\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\to Q^{\prime}\to 0,\chi(Q^{\prime})=\delta\}

where
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
is a line bundle on
\mathcal{C}_{b}
and
Q^{\prime}
is a zero-dimensional sheaf.
Since (
4.55
) attains the smallest in the right-hand side,
by Lemma
4.26
we have

(4.63)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\vee}\subset F_{0}^{\vee}\subset\nu_{*}\nu^{*}\mathcal{L}^{\vee}

where
\nu\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{b}\to\mathcal{C}_{b}
