is the normalization.
Since
\nu_{*}\nu^{*}\mathcal{L}^{\vee}/\mathcal{L}^{\vee}

is a direct sum of the form
k[t]/t^{m_{i}}
by Assumption
4.18
, there are only finitely many

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}_{b}}
-modules
F_{0}
satisfying (
4.63
). Therefore
q_{k}
is quasi-finite.
∎

The following lemma will be used in Lemma
4.24
:

Lemma 4.23
.

For any quasi-compact open substack
\mathcal{H}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}}\subset\mathcal{H}(\chi)
,
there is
d\gg 0
with
r|(d+\chi)
such that
the map
p_{k}
in the diagram (
4.5
)
is surjective on
\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{k}^{\mathrm{qc}}\subset\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\leqslant k}^{\mathrm{qc}}
.
Here
(-)^{\mathrm{qc}}
means the pull-back with respect to
\mathcal{H}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}}\subset\mathcal{H}(\chi)
.

Proof.

For
E\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{C}_{b})

corresponding to a point in
\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{k}^{\mathrm{qc}}
, there is an exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{L}\to E\to Q\to 0

as in
(
4.53
)
such that
\chi(Q)=k
. Note that
Q
is supported on
\mathcal{C}_{b}^{\mathrm{sg}}
,
as otherwise
E
lies in
\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\leqslant k-1}
.

Then
by setting

(4.64)

\displaystyle a=\frac{\chi-\chi_{0}+d}{r}\in\mathbb{Z},\ r|(d+\chi),\ d\gg 0

we have
an injection
\mathcal{L}(-a\cdot c)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}_{b}}

whose cokernel is supported on the smooth locus of
\mathcal{C}_{b}
,
as
\mathcal{L}^{\vee}(a\cdot c)
is very ample for
d\gg 0
.
Since
\mathcal{H}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}}
is quasi-compact, we can take
d\gg 0
to be independent of a point in
\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{k}^{\mathrm{qc}}
.
Then we have an injection
E(-a\cdot c)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}_{b}}

whose cokernel at singular points have length
k
, which implies the lemma.
∎

Lemma 4.24
.

Let
\mathcal{H}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}}\subset\mathcal{H}(\chi)
be a quasi-compact open substack.
Take
d\gg 0
as in Lemma
4.23
, and consider the open substack

\mathcal{U}_{\circ}^{\mathrm{qc}}\subset\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{qc}}
.
Then, for the morphism

p|_{\mathcal{U}_{\circ}^{\mathrm{qc}}}\colon\mathcal{U}_{\circ}^{\mathrm{qc}}\to\mathcal{H}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}},

the pullback functor

(p|_{\mathcal{U}_{\circ}^{\mathrm{qc}}})^{*}\colon\operatorname{QCoh}_{\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\geqslant k}^{\mathrm{qc}}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}})\to\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathcal{U}_{\circ}^{\mathrm{qc}})

is conservative on the open substack

\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{k}^{\mathrm{qc}}\subset\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\leqslant k}^{\mathrm{qc}}
.
That is, if an object

A\in\operatorname{QCoh}_{\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\geqslant k}^{\mathrm{qc}}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}})

is sent to zero under this functor, then
A
is supported on

\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\geqslant k+1}^{\mathrm{qc}}
.

Proof.

We have the factorization of the map
p\colon\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{H}(\chi)

\displaystyle p\colon\mathcal{U}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\beta}}{{\to}}\overline{\mathcal{U}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha}}{{\to}}\mathcal{H}(\chi)

where
\overline{\mathcal{U}}
is the moduli stack which classifies exact sequences in
\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(S)

(4.65)

\displaystyle 0\to F_{0}\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}_{b}}\to Q\to 0

where
Q
is zero-dimensional with length
d
. As a derived scheme, it is
equivalent to the derived moduli stack of PT stable pairs
[
36
,
37
]

(F_{0}^{\vee},\eta)
, where

F_{0}^{\vee}
is a pure one dimensional sheaf on
S
with support
\mathcal{C}_{b}
for
b\in\mathcal{B}
and

\eta\colon\mathcal{O}_{S}\to F_{0}^{\vee}
is generically
surjective whose cokernel has length
d
.
The map
\beta
sends (
4.51
)
to
F_{0}\subset F_{d}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}_{b}}
, and
\alpha

sends (
4.65
) to
F_{0}
.

By taking the pull-back along
\mathcal{H}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}}\subset\mathcal{H}(\chi)
, we obtain

\displaystyle p\colon\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{qc}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\beta}}{{\to}}\overline{\mathcal{U}}^{\mathrm{qc}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha}}{{\to}}\mathcal{H}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}}.

The map
\alpha
is a smooth map for
d\gg 0
.
Indeed for
E\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{C}_{b})
corresponding to a point in

\mathcal{H}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}}
, the fiber of
\alpha
is an open subset of

H^{0}(E^{\vee}(a\cdot c))
where
a
is given by (
4.64
),
whose dimension is constant for
d\gg 0
as
\mathcal{H}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}}
is quasi-compact.
Therefore
\overline{\mathcal{U}}^{\mathrm{qc}}
is an open substack of a vector bundle over

\mathcal{H}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}}
, hence
\alpha
is smooth.

We have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{qc}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{U}}^{\mathrm{qc}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{C})^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{B}}^{d}(\mathcal{C}).}

Here
\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{B}}^{d}(\mathcal{C})
is the relative symmetric product of
\mathcal{C}
over
\mathcal{B}
.
We take the open subset

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{B}}^{d}(\mathcal{C})_{\leqslant k}\subset\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{B}}^{d}(\mathcal{C})

to be consisting of unordered tuples
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})

with
x_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{b}
such that the number of
1\leqslant i\leqslant d
with
x_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{b}^{\mathrm{sg}}
is at most
k
.
We denote by
\overline{\mathcal{U}}_{\circ}^{\mathrm{qc}}\subset\overline{\mathcal{U}}^{\mathrm{qc}}

the pull-back with respect to the above open subset.
Then the diagram (
4.5
) restricts to the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{qc}}_{\circ}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{\circ}}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{U}}^{\mathrm{qc}}_{\circ}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{C})^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}_{\leqslant k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{B}}^{d}(\mathcal{C})_{\leqslant k}.}

We obtain the diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{qc}}_{\circ}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{\circ}}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{U}}^{\mathrm{qc}}_{\circ}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{\circ}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{qc}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{U}}^{\mathrm{qc}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}}.}

By Lemma
4.23
and the property (
4.62
),
the map
\alpha_{\circ}
is surjective on
\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{k}^{\mathrm{qc}}
.
This implies that the functor

(4.74)

\displaystyle\alpha_{\circ}^{*}\colon\operatorname{QCoh}_{\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\geqslant k}^{\mathrm{qc}}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}})\to\operatorname{QCoh}(\overline{\mathcal{U}}_{\circ}^{\mathrm{qc}})

is conservative in a neighborhood of
\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{k}^{\mathrm{qc}}
.
Indeed as
\alpha_{\circ}
is smooth, there is an open substack

\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{H}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}}

such that

(4.75)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{H}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}}_{\geqslant k}=\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{k}^{\mathrm{qc}}

and the map

\alpha_{\circ}^{-1}(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})\to\mathcal{H}^{\prime}
is smooth and surjective, in particular faithfully flat.
Then the functor

(4.76)

\displaystyle\alpha_{\circ}^{*}\colon\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})\to\operatorname{QCoh}(\alpha_{\circ}^{-1}(\mathcal{H}^{\prime}))

is conservative. Now if
A\in\operatorname{QCoh}_{\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\geqslant k}^{\mathrm{qc}}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}})

is sent to zero under the functor (
4.74
),
then
\alpha_{\circ}^{*}A|_{\alpha_{\circ}^{-1}(\mathcal{H}^{\prime})}=0
, hence

A|_{\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}=0
as (
4.76
) is conservative.
Therefore
A
is supported on
\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\geqslant k+1}^{\mathrm{qc}}
by the condition (
4.75
).

It remains to show that the functor

\displaystyle\beta_{\circ}^{*}\colon\operatorname{QCoh}(\overline{\mathcal{U}}_{\circ}^{\mathrm{qc}})\to\operatorname{QCoh}(\mathcal{U}_{\circ}^{\mathrm{qc}})

is conservative.
Below we use the short hand notation of (nested) Hilbert schemes of points, see the proof of Proposition
3.7

\displaystyle H^{d}=\operatorname{Hilb}^{d}(S),\ H^{d,d-1}=\operatorname{Hilb}^{d,d-1}(S).

We define

\displaystyle H^{1_{d}}:=H^{d,d-1}\times_{H^{d-1}}H^{d-1,d-2}\times_{H^{d-2}}\cdots\times_{H^{2}}H^{2,1}.

It is the derived moduli stack which classifies flags of zero-dimensional subschemes

(4.77)

\displaystyle Z_{1}\subset Z_{2}\subset\cdots\subset Z_{d}\subset S

where
Z_{i}
has length
i
.
Then we have the Cartesian square

\textstyle{\mathcal{U}_{\circ}^{\mathrm{qc}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{\circ}}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{U}}_{\circ}^{\mathrm{qc}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}\times H^{1_{d}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1\times\beta_{H}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}\times H^{d}.}

Here vertical arrows are closed embeddings, and the bottom horizontal arrow
sends (
4.77
) to
Z_{d}\subset S
.
Since the push-forward functors of quasi-coherent sheaves along with the vertical arrows are conservative,
by the base change it is enough to show that

(4.78)

\displaystyle\beta_{H}^{*}\colon\operatorname{QCoh}(H^{d})\to\operatorname{QCoh}(H^{1_{d}})

is conservative. It is proved in Lemma
4.25
.
∎

Lemma 4.25
.

The functor (
4.78
) is conservative.

Proof.

Let
X^{d}=\widetilde{\operatorname{Hilb}}^{d}(S)
be the isospectral Hilbert scheme, see Subsection
3.5
. We show that, by the induction on
d
, that
there is a factorization of the projection
H^{1_{d}}\to H^{d}

(4.79)

\displaystyle H^{1_{d}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f^{d}}}{{\to}}X^{d}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rho^{d}}}{{\to}}H^{d}

such that
f^{d}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{H^{1_{d}}}=\mathcal{O}_{X^{d}}
.
The case of
d=1
is obvious; we can take
f^{1}=\operatorname{id}
.

Assume that
f^{d-1}
is constructed. We have the following Cartesian squares

\textstyle{H^{1_{d}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h^{d}}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\textstyle{Y^{d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\textstyle{H^{d,d-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1_{d-1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{d-1}}
\textstyle{X^{d-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{d-1}}
\textstyle{H^{d-1}.}

Here
Y^{d}
satisfies that

\displaystyle Y^{d}=(Y^{d})^{\mathrm{cl}}=(Y^{d})^{\mathrm{cl,red}}.

The first identity follows since
\rho^{d-1}
is flat (which is the key property of
isospectral Hilbert scheme
[
19
]
), and the second identity is
also proved in the proof of
[
19
, Lemma 3.8.5]
.
Then we have the induced morphism

g^{d}\colon Y^{d}\to X^{d}
, and it satisfies

g^{d}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y^{d}}=\mathcal{O}_{X^{d}}
, see the proof of
[
19
, Lemma 3.8.5]
.
By the diagram (
4.5
), derived base change and induction hypothesis,
we have
h^{d}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{H^{1_{d}}}=\mathcal{O}_{Y^{d}}
.
Therefore the map

\displaystyle f^{d}=g^{d}\circ h^{d}\colon H^{1_{d}}\to Y^{d}\to X^{d}

satisfies the desired property.

The functor

\displaystyle(\rho^{d})^{*}\colon\operatorname{QCoh}(H^{d})\to\operatorname{QCoh}(X^{d})

is conservative as
\rho^{d}
is finite flat cover of
H^{d}
, hence faithfully flat.
The functor

\displaystyle(f^{d})^{*}\colon\operatorname{QCoh}(X^{d})\to\operatorname{QCoh}(H^{1_{d}})

is conservative (indeed fully-faithful) since it admits a right adjoint

(f^{d})_{*}
satisfying

(f^{d})_{*}(f^{d})^{*}=\operatorname{id}
because of
f^{d}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{H^{1_{d}}}=\mathcal{O}_{X^{d}}
.
Therefore by the factorization (
4.79
), the functor (
4.78
) is conservative.
∎

The following is probably well known and follows from a standard argument.
We will give its proof in Subsection
6.4
because we cannot find a reference.

Lemma 4.26
.

Let
R=k[[x,y]]/(f)
be a planar singularity, and

M
be a rank one torsion-free
R
-module. Let

\displaystyle\delta:=\min\{\dim M/R:R\hookrightarrow M\}

where the minimum is after all injective

R
-module homomorphisms
R\hookrightarrow M
,
so that
M/R
is finite dimensional. Then if an
embedding
R\hookrightarrow M

satisfies
\delta=\dim M/R
, then
we have

\displaystyle R\hookrightarrow M\hookrightarrow\widetilde{R}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{\ell}k[[t_{i}]]

where
\operatorname{Spec}\widetilde{R}\to\operatorname{Spec}R
is the normalization.

Remark 4.27
.

If
\mathcal{C}_{b}
has at worst nodal singularities, then

\mathcal{H}(\chi)_{\leqslant k}
corresponds to
E\in\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{C}_{b})
such
that the number of points in
\mathcal{C}_{b}
at which
E
is not a line
bundle is at most
k
.

As a corollary of the above proposition together with Theorem
2.8
, we have the following:

Corollary 4.28
.

Under Assumption
4.18
,
for
d\gg 0
the image of the functor

\displaystyle\operatorname{Coh}(S^{\times d})\to\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0}-d))_{w^{\prime}}

given by

(4.84)

\displaystyle A_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes A_{d}\mapsto\mathrm{H}_{A_{d}}\circ\cdots\circ\mathrm{H}_{A_{1}}(s_{!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}})

compactly generates
\operatorname{IndL}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0}-d))_{w^{\prime}}
.

4.6.
Equivalence of categories

The following is the main result in this section:

Theorem 4.29
.

Under Conjecture
4.4

the functor
\Phi
in (
2.31
) is fully-faithful

(4.85)

\displaystyle\Phi\colon\operatorname{IndCoh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{IndL}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))_{w^{\prime}}.

Moreover, under Assumption
4.18
,the functor (
4.85
) is an equivalence
i.e. the DL holds over
\mathcal{B}
.

Proof.

We consider the adjoint pair of functors in Corollary
4.11

\textstyle{\operatorname{IndCoh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{\operatorname{IndL}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))_{w^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi^{R}}

We have the distinguished triangle of functors

(4.88)

\displaystyle\operatorname{id}\to\Phi^{R}\Phi\to\Upsilon

where the first arrow is given by the adjunction.
By applying
\mathrm{W}_{d,c}^{R}
, we obtain

(4.89)

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{d,c}^{R}\to\mathrm{W}_{d,c}^{R}\Phi^{R}\Phi\to\mathrm{W}_{d,c}^{R}\Upsilon.

By the diagram in Corollary
4.11
, we have

(4.90)

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{d,c}^{R}\Phi^{R}\Phi\cong\mathrm{H}_{d,c}^{R}\Phi\cong\mathrm{W}_{d,c}^{R}

so that the distinguished triangle (
4.89
) is of the form

(4.91)

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{d,c}^{R}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\gamma}}{{\to}}\mathrm{W}_{d,c}^{R}\to\mathrm{W}_{d,c}^{R}\Upsilon.

We show that
\gamma=\operatorname{id}
. The kernel object of
\mathrm{W}_{d,c}^{R}
is

\mathcal{E}^{(d)}[d]
, where
\mathcal{E}^{(d)}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(d)}:=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{d}(p_{i}\times\operatorname{id})^{*}\mathcal{E}^{\vee}\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(S^{\times d}\times\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}).

Here
p_{i}
is the projection from
S^{\times d}
onto the corresponding factor.
Since
\mathcal{E}^{\vee}
is a
\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}
-flat family of one-dimensional
sheaves on
S
with support in
\mathcal{C}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}
,
the sheaf
\mathcal{E}^{(d)}
is flat over
\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}
and supported over

\mathcal{C}^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}
. Then
(\gamma-\operatorname{id})[-d]
is induced by a
morphism of
\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}
-flat sheaves
\mathcal{E}^{(d)}\to\mathcal{E}^{(d)}
, see Lemma
6.1
.
Since
h\colon\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}\to\mathcal{B}
is also flat by
[
7
, Theorem 2.2.4]
,
the sheaf
\mathcal{E}^{(d)}
is also flat over
\mathcal{B}
.

On the other hand, the isomorphisms (
4.90
) are induced from the isomorphism (
3.75
), which is canonical by its construction. Over

\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{sm}}:=\mathcal{B}\cap\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{sm}}
, we can identify
\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}
with the relative
Jacobian and
\mathcal{E}^{\vee}
with the dual of Poincaré line bundle. From the above description it is straightforward to check that, over
\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{sm}}
, we have
\gamma|_{S^{\times d}\times\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{sm}}}=\operatorname{id}
.

This implies that, if
\gamma\neq\operatorname{id}
, then the image of
(\gamma-\operatorname{id})[-d]
is a non-zero subsheaf
of
\mathcal{E}^{(d)}
supported over

\mathcal{C}^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{sg}}
,
where
\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{sg}}:=\mathcal{B}\setminus\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{sm}}
. However, as
\mathcal{B}
is smooth and irreducible and
\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{sg}}\subsetneq\mathcal{B}
is a closed subset, such a subsheaf is annihilated by

I^{N}
for
N\gg 0
, where
I\subset\mathcal{B}
is the ideal sheaf which defines
\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{sg}}
.
This contradicts that
\mathcal{E}^{(d)}
is flat over
\mathcal{B}
, therefore
\gamma=\operatorname{id}
.

As
\gamma=\operatorname{id}
, from the triangle (
4.91
) we have
\mathrm{W}_{d,c}^{R}\Upsilon=0
.
Then by Corollary
4.16
, we have
\Upsilon=0
. Therefore from
(
4.88
), we have the isomorphism

\operatorname{id}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}\Phi^{R}\Phi
, i.e.
\Phi
is fully-faithful.

The objects of the form (
4.84
) come from the image of the functor (
4.85
)
by Corollary
4.10
. Therefore by Corollary
4.28
, under Assumption
4.18
,
the fully-faithful functor (
4.85
) is also essentially surjective, hence an equivalence.
∎

Remark 4.30
.

In the proof of Theorem
4.29
, we have omitted writing down the
details on the check of

\gamma|_{S^{\times d}\times\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{sm}}}=\operatorname{id}
. Instead
in Subsection
6.6
we give another proof without relying on it,
which however works except
g=2
and
G=\operatorname{GL}_{2}
.

5.
Whittaker normalization for
G=\operatorname{GL}_{2}

In this section, we restrict to the case of
G=\operatorname{GL}_{2}
and show that
Conjecture
4.4
holds in this case, see Proposition
5.12
.
Together with Theorem
4.29
and Example
4.19
(1),
we obtain the main result:

Theorem 5.1
.

The DL holds over
\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{red}}\subset\mathrm{B}
for
G=\operatorname{GL}_{2}
.

Below we assume that
G=\operatorname{GL}_{2}
. By Remark
2.13
, it is enough to prove
the case of

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}=\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{red,cl}}\subset\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{cl}}.

As in the previous sections, we use the notation

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}=\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}\times_{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{B}.

Recall that we have the universal families

(5.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{C}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}),\ \mathcal{F}\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(C\times\mathcal{H}).

They are related by
\pi_{*}\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{F}
, where

\pi\colon\mathcal{C}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}\to C\times\mathcal{H}
is the projection.

We take
b\in\mathcal{B}
such that the corresponding spectral curve
\mathcal{C}_{b}\subset S
is not irreducible; so it is of the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{b}=C_{1}\cup C_{2},\ C_{1}\cap C_{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}m_{i}x_{i}

for
x_{i}\in C_{1}\cap C_{2}
such that
m_{1}+\cdots+m_{\ell}=2g-2
, and
C_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}C
.

5.1.
Resolution of Arinkin sheaf

Let
E\in\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{C}_{b})
be a rank one (not necessarily semistable)
torsion-free sheaf. As in (
3.40
),
the pull-back of the Arinkin sheaf (
2.28
) yields
the Cohen–Macaulay sheaf

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{E}\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{H}_{b}).

Below we construct a resolution of the above sheaf by vector bundles, which will be used to show that it lies in the limit category.

Lemma 5.2
.

There is an exact sequence
of the form

(5.2)

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{L}\to E\to Q\to 0

where
\mathcal{L}
is a line bundle on
\mathcal{C}_{b}
and

Q\cong\oplus_{i=1}^{\ell}\mathcal{O}_{Z_{i}}
, where

Z_{i}
is a closed subscheme

\displaystyle Z_{i}\hookrightarrow(C_{1}\cap C_{2})_{x_{i}}=\operatorname{Spec}k[t]/t^{m_{i}},

i.e.
Z_{i}=\operatorname{Spec}k[t]/t^{n_{i}}
for some
0\leqslant n_{i}\leqslant m_{i}
.

Proof.

From Lemma
4.26
, there is a line bundle
\mathcal{L}
with
an injection
\mathcal{L}\hookrightarrow E
such that

E/\mathcal{L}\subset\nu_{*}\nu^{*}\mathcal{L}
, where

\nu\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{b}\to\mathcal{C}_{b}
is the normalization.
Then the lemma follows from the fact that

\nu_{*}\nu^{*}\mathcal{L}/\mathcal{L}
is supported at
\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{\ell}\}
, where at each
x_{i}
its stalk is
isomorphic to
k[t]/t^{m_{i}}
.
∎

We choose a surjection
\mathcal{O}_{Z_{1}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{x_{1}}
and consider the
exact sequence

(5.3)

\displaystyle 0\to E^{\prime}\to E\to\mathcal{O}_{x_{1}}\to 0.

Here
E^{\prime}
fits into an exact sequence

(5.4)

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{L}\to E^{\prime}\to Q^{\prime}\to 0

such that
\chi(Q^{\prime})=\chi(Q)-1
.
The sequence (
5.3
) corresponds to a map

(5.5)

\displaystyle\tau_{0}\colon\operatorname{Spec}k\to\mathcal{H}ecke:=\mathrm{Hecke}_{G}\times_{\mathrm{B}}\mathcal{B}.

Here
\mathrm{Hecke}_{G}
is the stack of Hecke correspondences, see Subsection
3.2
.

Lemma 5.3
.

For each
D\geqslant 1
, there is a pointed smooth affine variety
(\Delta,0)
for
0\in\Delta
with
\dim\Delta=D

and an extension of the map (
5.5
)

(5.6)

\displaystyle\tau\colon\Delta\to\mathcal{H}ecke,\ \tau|_{0}=\tau_{0}

such that the composition

\displaystyle\Delta\to\mathcal{H}ecke\to\mathcal{B}

sends the generic point of
\Delta
to
\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{sm}}\subset\mathrm{B}
, the open subset
corresponding to smooth spectral curves.

Proof.

It is enough to show that each irreducible component of
\mathcal{H}ecke
is
dominant onto
\mathcal{B}
in a neighborhood of
b\in\mathcal{B}

under the map
\mathcal{H}ecke\to\mathcal{B}
.
We have the factorization

(5.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}ecke\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{ev}_{2}}}{{\to}}\mathcal{H}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h}}{{\to}}\mathcal{B}.

The map
h
is flat and surjective, see
[
7
, Theorem 2.2.4]
.
On the other hand,
the virtual dimension of a fiber of
\mathrm{ev}_{2}
is one by Lemma
3.1
, and it equals
the dimension of its classical truncation.
Indeed from the exact sequence (
5.2
), we see that
\operatorname{Hom}(E,\mathcal{O}_{x})
is at most two-dimensional, hence
\operatorname{Hom}(E,\mathcal{O}_{x})/\mathbb{G}_{m}
for the scaling action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
is at most one-dimensional.
It follows that the fibers of

\mathrm{ev}_{2}
are one-dimensional on
h^{-1}(U)
for an open neighborhood
b\in U\subset\mathcal{B}
. Therefore the fibers of (
5.7
) are classical
in a neighborhood of
b\in\mathcal{B}
; this does not happen if
there is an irreducible component of
\mathcal{H}ecke
which is not
dominant onto
\mathcal{B}
in a neighborhood of
b\in\mathcal{B}
.
Therefore we obtain the lemma.
∎

The map (
5.6
) corresponds to an exact sequence
in
\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{C}_{\Delta})

where
\mathcal{C}_{\Delta}:=\mathcal{C}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\Delta

(5.8)

\displaystyle 0\to E_{\Delta}^{\prime}\to E_{\Delta}\to R_{\Delta}\to 0

such that each term is flat over
\Delta
, and restricts to the
sequence (
5.3
) at
0\in\Delta
.

Let
\tau_{i}
for
i=1,2
be the composition

\displaystyle\tau_{i}\colon\Delta\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tau}}{{\to}}\mathcal{H}ecke\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{ev}_{i}}}{{\to}}\mathcal{H}.

We set
\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}:=\Delta\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}
and define

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\Delta i}:=(\tau_{i}\times\operatorname{id})^{*}\mathcal{P}^{\flat}\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}),\ i=1,2.

Here
\mathcal{P}^{\flat}
is the Cohen–Macaulay sheaf in Theorem
2.16
.
Since
\mathcal{P}^{\flat}
is flat over both factors of
\mathcal{H}
,
the sheaves
\mathcal{P}_{\Delta i}
are
Cohen–Macaulay sheaves on
\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}
flat over
\Delta
,
such that
\mathcal{P}_{\Delta 2}|_{0}\cong\mathcal{P}_{E}
and

\mathcal{P}_{\Delta 1}|_{0}\cong\mathcal{P}_{E^{\prime}}
.

We also consider the involution
\sigma
on
\mathcal{H}

given by

\displaystyle(F,\theta)\mapsto(F,-\theta+\mathrm{tr}(\theta)\cdot 1_{F}).

Geometrically, it corresponds to the pull-back under the covering involution of the
spectral curve
\mathcal{C}_{b}\to C
, in particular
\sigma
preserves the Hitchin fibration
h
.
We define

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\Delta i}^{\sigma}:=\sigma^{*}\mathcal{P}_{\Delta i}\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}).

The sheaf
R_{\Delta}
in (
5.8
) is a
\Delta
-flat family of
skyscraper sheaves of points on the fibers of
\mathcal{C}_{\Delta}\to\Delta
.
Therefore it determines a closed immersion

\displaystyle\eta\colon\Delta\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\Delta}

corresponding to the support of
R_{\Delta}\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{C}_{\Delta})
.
We have the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\Delta\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{\prime\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\Delta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{C\times\Delta\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{C.}

Here the bottom arrows are projections.
By taking
\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}
, it also induces the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{\prime\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\Delta}\times_{\Delta}\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{C\times\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{C\times\mathcal{H}.}

Here we have used the same symbols as in (
5.1
) by abuse of notation.
We set
\mathcal{F}_{\Delta}:=(\eta^{{}^{\prime\prime}})^{*}\mathcal{F}
, which is a rank two
vector bundle on
\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}.

We have the following proposition:

Proposition 5.4
.

There is an exact sequence of vector bundles on
\Delta\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{reg}}

(5.17)

\displaystyle 0\to j^{\mathrm{reg}*}\mathcal{P}_{\Delta 2}^{\sigma}\to j^{\mathrm{reg}*}\mathcal{F}_{\Delta}\otimes j^{\mathrm{reg}*}\mathcal{P}_{\Delta 1}\to j^{\mathrm{reg}*}\mathcal{P}_{\Delta 2}\to 0.

Here
j^{\mathrm{reg}}
is the open immersion

\displaystyle j^{\mathrm{reg}}\colon\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{reg}}_{\Delta}:=\Delta\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{reg}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}.

Proof.

We have the following diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{reg}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\Delta}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\Delta}\times_{\Delta}\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{reg}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{p_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{reg}}.}

Here bottom arrows are projections. Let

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{C}_{\Delta}\times_{\Delta}\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{reg}})

be the pull-back of the universal family (
5.1
)
via
\mathcal{C}_{\Delta}\times_{\Delta}\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{reg}}\to\mathcal{C}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}
, which is a line bundle by the definition of
\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{reg}}
.
Then by the construction and using (
5.8
), we have an isomorphism
of line bundles on
\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{reg}}

\displaystyle j^{\mathrm{reg}*}\mathcal{P}_{\Delta 2}

\displaystyle=\det p_{2*}(p_{1}^{*}E_{\Delta}\otimes\widetilde{\mathcal{E}})\otimes(\det p_{2*}p_{1}^{*}E_{\Delta})^{-1}\otimes(\det p_{2*}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}})^{-1}\otimes\det p_{2*}\mathcal{O}

\displaystyle\cong j^{\mathrm{reg}*}\mathcal{P}_{\Delta 1}\otimes\det p_{2*}(p_{1}^{*}R_{\Delta}\otimes\widetilde{\mathcal{E}})

(5.18)

\displaystyle\cong j^{\mathrm{reg}*}\mathcal{P}_{\Delta 1}\otimes\eta^{*}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}.

Below we use the same notation as in (
5.1
) for the
restricted diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{reg}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{\prime\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\Delta}\times_{\Delta}\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{reg}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{C\times\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{reg}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{C\times\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{reg}}.}

The map
\pi
in the diagram (
5.1
) is a
2\colon 1
cover with covering involution
\sigma
, and we have the exact sequence in
\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{C}_{\Delta})
.

\displaystyle 0\to\sigma_{*}\eta_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\Delta}\to\pi^{*}\pi_{*}\eta_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\Delta}\to\eta_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\Delta}\to 0.

By pulling it back to
\mathcal{C}_{\Delta}\times_{\Delta}\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{reg}}

and applying
\otimes\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}
,
we obtain the exact sequence in
\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{C}_{\Delta}\times_{\Delta}\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{reg}})

\displaystyle 0\to p_{1}^{*}\sigma_{*}\eta_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\Delta}\otimes\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\to p_{1}^{*}\pi^{*}\pi_{*}\eta_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\Delta}\otimes\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\to p_{1}^{*}\eta_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\Delta}\otimes\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\to 0.

Then applying
\pi_{*}
in the diagram (
5.1
), we obtain
the exact sequence in
\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(C\times\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{reg}})

\displaystyle 0\to\pi_{*}(p_{1}^{*}\sigma_{*}\eta_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\Delta}\otimes\widetilde{\mathcal{E}})\to\pi_{*}(p_{1}^{*}\pi^{*}\pi_{*}\eta_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\Delta}\otimes\widetilde{\mathcal{E}})\to\pi_{*}(p_{1}^{*}\eta_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\Delta}\otimes\widetilde{\mathcal{E}})\to 0.

By applying the projection formula and noting that

(\eta^{\prime})^{*}\pi_{*}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\cong j^{\mathrm{reg}*}\mathcal{F}_{\Delta}
,
the above exact sequence is identified with

\displaystyle 0\to\eta^{{}^{\prime}}_{*}(\sigma\circ\eta)^{*}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\to\eta^{{}^{\prime}}_{*}j^{\mathrm{reg}*}\mathcal{F}_{\Delta}\to\eta^{{}^{\prime}}_{*}\eta^{*}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\to 0.

Since
\eta^{\prime}
is a closed immersion (as it is a section of the projection)
we obtain the exact sequence in
\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{reg}})

\displaystyle 0\to(\sigma\circ\eta)^{*}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\to j^{\mathrm{reg}*}\mathcal{F}_{\Delta}\to\eta^{*}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\to 0.

By applying
j^{\mathrm{reg}*}\mathcal{P}_{\Delta 1}\otimes
and using (
5.18
), we
obtain the desired exact sequence (
5.17
).
∎

As a corollary of the above proposition, we have the following:

Corollary 5.5
.

The exact sequence (
5.17
) extends to a left exact sequence of maximal Cohen–Macaulay
sheaves on
\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}

(5.23)

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{P}_{\Delta 2}^{\sigma}\to\mathcal{F}_{\Delta}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{\Delta 1}\to\mathcal{P}_{\Delta 2}.

Proof.

Let
j_{*}^{\mathrm{reg}\heartsuit}
be the functor

\displaystyle j_{*}^{\mathrm{reg}\heartsuit}:=\mathcal{H}^{0}(j_{*}^{\mathrm{reg}})\colon\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{reg}})\to\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}).

The above functor is left exact.
By the condition
that
\Delta\to\mathcal{B}
sends the generic point to the locus of
smooth spectral curves, the open immersion
\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{reg}}\subset\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}
is an isomorphism in codimension one. Then as
\mathcal{P}_{\Delta i}
are
maximal Cohen–Macaulay sheaves and
\mathcal{F}_{\Delta}
is a vector bundle, we have

j_{*}^{\mathrm{reg}\heartsuit}j^{\mathrm{reg}*}\mathcal{P}_{\Delta i}\cong\mathcal{P}_{\Delta i}

and
j_{*}^{\mathrm{reg}\heartsuit}j^{\mathrm{reg}*}\mathcal{F}_{\Delta}\cong\mathcal{F}_{\Delta}
.
Therefore by applying
j_{*}^{\mathrm{reg}\heartsuit}
to (
5.17
),
we obtain the left exact sequence (
5.23
).
∎

In particular by taking the restriction of (
5.23
) over
0\in\Delta
, we obtain
the sequence in
\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{H}_{b})

(5.24)

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{P}_{E}^{\sigma}\to\mathcal{F}_{c}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{E^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{P}_{E}

which at this stage is neither left exact nor right exact.
Here
\mathcal{P}_{E}^{\sigma}:=\sigma^{*}\mathcal{P}_{E}
,

c=\pi(x_{1})\in C
and
\mathcal{F}_{c}:=\mathcal{F}|_{c\times\mathcal{H}_{b}}
.
In what follows, we show that the sequence (
5.23
) is right exact, which in particular implies that (
5.24
) is an
exact sequence.
We first show this in the case that
\mathcal{C}_{b}
has at worst nodal singularities.

Lemma 5.6
.

If
\mathcal{C}_{b}
has at worst nodal singularities, the
sequence (
5.23
) is also right exact. In particular, the sequence (
5.24
) is an exact sequence.

Proof.

The assumption on
\mathcal{C}_{b}
implies that

m_{i}=1
and
the sheaf
E^{\prime}
on
\mathcal{C}_{b}
is a line bundle at
x_{1}
. Let
\mathcal{C}_{\Delta}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{C}_{\Delta}
be an open neighborhood of
x_{1}\in\mathcal{C}_{b}=\mathcal{C}_{\Delta}\times_{\Delta}0
such that

E_{t}^{\prime}
is a line bundle on
\mathcal{C}_{\Delta}^{\prime}\times_{\Delta}t

for all
t\in\Delta
.
Then there is a morphism

(5.25)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\Delta}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}

sending
x\in\mathcal{C}_{\Delta}^{\prime}\times_{\Delta}t

to
E_{x}\in\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{C}_{t})
which fits into
the unique non-trivial extension (up to isomorphisms)

\displaystyle 0\to E_{t}^{\prime}\to E_{x}\to\mathcal{O}_{x}\to 0.

On the other hand, since
\eta(0)=x_{1}\in\mathcal{C}_{\Delta}^{\prime}
,
by shrinking
\Delta
if necessary we may assume that

\eta\colon\Delta\to\mathcal{C}_{\Delta}
factors through

\eta\colon\Delta\to\mathcal{C}_{\Delta}^{\prime}
. We define

\widetilde{\Delta}
to be the Cartesian square

\textstyle{\widetilde{\Delta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\Delta}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\Delta\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\Delta}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{C\times\Delta.}

The map
\pi\colon\widetilde{\Delta}\to\Delta
is a
2\colon 1
cover
with involution
\sigma
together with the image of a section
\Delta\subset\widetilde{\Delta}
induced by
\eta
such that

\widetilde{\Delta}=\Delta\cup\sigma(\Delta)

and
\Delta\cap\sigma(\Delta)
is a divisor in
\Delta
. By shrinking

\Delta
if necessary, we may assume that
\mathcal{O}_{\Delta}(\Delta\cap\sigma(\Delta))
is trivial, so that we have the exact sequence

(5.30)

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{O}_{\sigma(\Delta)}\to\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\Delta}}\to\mathcal{O}_{\Delta}\to 0.

By composing (
5.25
) with the top horizontal arrow in (
5.1
),
we obtain the map

\displaystyle\widetilde{\tau}\colon\widetilde{\Delta}\to\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}.

It yields the maximal Cohen–Macaulay sheaf

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\widetilde{\Delta}}:=(\widetilde{\tau}\times\operatorname{id})^{*}\mathcal{P}^{\flat}\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{H}_{\widetilde{\Delta}})

Here
\widetilde{\tau}\times\operatorname{id}
is

\displaystyle\widetilde{\tau}\times\operatorname{id}\colon\mathcal{H}_{\widetilde{\Delta}}=\widetilde{\Delta}\times_{\Delta}\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}\to\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}\times_{\Delta}\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}\to\mathcal{H}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}.

The exact sequence (
5.30
) then induces the exact
sequence in
\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{H}_{\Delta})

(5.31)

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{P}_{\Delta 2}^{\sigma}\to\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\mathcal{P}_{\widetilde{\Delta}}\to\mathcal{P}_{\Delta 2}\to 0.

Here we have used the same notation
\widetilde{\pi}
for the
map

\mathcal{H}_{\widetilde{\Delta}}\to\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}
.

Unraveling the construction, the above exact sequence restricted to

\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{reg}}
is identified with (
5.17
).
Since
\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{reg}}\subset\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}
is an isomorphism in codimension one, and both of
\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\mathcal{P}_{\widetilde{\Delta}}
and
\mathcal{F}_{\Delta}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{\Delta 1}
are maximal Cohen–Macaulay sheaves, we
conclude that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\mathcal{P}_{\widetilde{\Delta}}\cong\mathcal{F}_{\Delta}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{\Delta 1}.

Therefore from the exact sequence (
5.31
), we obtain the lemma.

∎

Using the above lemma for the nodal case, we prove that (
5.24
) is exact
in general:

Proposition 5.7
.

The sequence (
5.24
) is an exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{P}_{E}^{\sigma}\to\mathcal{F}_{c}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{E^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{P}_{E}\to 0.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{sg}}\subset\mathcal{B}
be the closed subset corresponding
to the singular spectral curves. It is of codimension one, and its
generic point corresponds to those with at worst nodal singularities.
Then we can take
\Delta
so that
\dim\Delta=2
and
the image of the composition

\displaystyle\Delta\setminus\{0\}\hookrightarrow\Delta\to\mathcal{B}

corresponds to the locus of spectral curves which are either smooth or have at worst nodal singularities. Then from Lemma
5.6
, the sequence (
5.23
) is
exact on
\Delta\setminus\{0\}
.

Assume that the sequence (
5.23
) is not exact.
Let
\mathbb{P}_{\Delta}
be the complex (
5.23
)
regarded as an object in
\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}_{\Delta})
, where

\mathcal{P}_{\Delta 2}
is located in degree zero.
Since (
5.23
) is left exact, the object
\mathbb{P}_{\Delta}
is a non-zero object
in
\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{H}_{\Delta})
and supported
over
0\in\Delta
.
Let
i\colon\Delta^{\prime}\subset\Delta
be a generic smooth divisor
such that
0\in\Delta^{\prime}
.
We use the same notation
i
for the induced map
i\colon\mathcal{H}_{\Delta^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}
.
Then as
\mathbb{P}_{\Delta}
is supported over
0\in\Delta^{\prime}
,
it follows that

\displaystyle 0\neq\mathcal{H}^{-1}(i^{*}\mathbb{P}_{\Delta})\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{H}_{\Delta^{\prime}}).

On the other hand,
since each term of
\mathbb{P}_{\Delta}
is flat over
\Delta
,
we have
i^{*}\mathbb{P}_{\Delta}=\mathbb{P}_{\Delta^{\prime}}
and it
lies in
\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{H}_{\Delta^{\prime}})
by
the left exactness of
the sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{P}_{\Delta^{\prime}2}^{\sigma}\to\mathcal{F}_{\Delta^{\prime}}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{\Delta^{\prime}1}\to\mathcal{P}_{\Delta^{\prime}2}.

which is obtained as in
(
5.23
) by replacing
\Delta
with
\Delta^{\prime}
.
It contradicts
\mathcal{H}^{-1}(i^{*}\mathbb{P}_{\Delta})\neq 0
,
hence the sequence (
5.23
) is exact.
Then by taking the restriction of (
5.23
) to
0\in\Delta
,
we conclude that the sequence (
5.24
) is exact.
∎

Recall the line bundle
\mathcal{L}
on
\mathcal{C}_{b}
in
Lemma
5.2
. As a corollary, we obtain the following explicit
resolution of
\mathcal{P}_{E}
in terms of vector bundles:

Corollary 5.8
.

Let
c_{i}=\pi(x_{i})\in C
, and
\mathcal{V}_{E}
be the following
vector bundle on
\mathcal{H}_{b}

(5.32)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{E}:=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{\ell}\mathcal{F}_{c_{i}}^{\otimes n_{i}}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{L}}.

There is a left resolution of
\mathcal{P}_{E}
in
\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{H}_{b})

of the form for some
k_{i},k_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 1}

\displaystyle\cdots\to\mathcal{V}_{E}^{\oplus k_{2}}\oplus\sigma^{*}\mathcal{V}_{E}^{\oplus k_{2}^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{V}_{E}^{\oplus k_{1}}\oplus\sigma^{*}\mathcal{V}_{E}^{\oplus k_{1}^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{P}_{E}\to 0.

Proof.

We prove the corollary by the induction on
\chi(Q)\geqslant 0
, where
Q
is given in Lemma
5.2
.
The base case,
\chi(Q)=0
, is obvious since
E=\mathcal{L}
in this case.

Suppose that
\chi(Q)>0
.
By Proposition
5.7
and noting that
\sigma^{*}\mathcal{F}_{c}\cong\mathcal{F}_{c}
,
there is an exact sequence of the form

\displaystyle\cdots\to\mathcal{F}_{c_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{E^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{F}_{c_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{E^{\prime}}^{\sigma}\to\mathcal{F}_{c_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{E^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{P}_{E}\to 0.

Since
\chi(Q^{\prime})=\chi(Q)-1
in the sequence (
5.4
),
we can apply the induction hypothesis for
\mathcal{P}_{E^{\prime}}
. Then the claim
for
\mathcal{P}_{E}
holds by the above exact sequence together with

\mathcal{F}_{c_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{V}_{E^{\prime}}\cong\mathcal{V}_{E}
.
∎

5.2.
Arinkin sheaf and limit category

For
E\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{C}_{b})
,
let
\deg\pi_{*}E=w
so that
\deg_{\mathcal{C}_{b}}(E)=w^{\prime}:=w+2g-2
.
In this subsection, using the resolution of the Arinkin sheaf in the previous
subsection, we show that it lies in the limit category.
Let
\mathcal{V}_{E}
be the vector bundle given by (
5.32
).
We prove the following proposition:

Proposition 5.9
.

Suppose that
E\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{C}_{b})
is semistable. Then we have

(5.33)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{E}\in\widetilde{\operatorname{L}}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}_{b})_{w^{\prime}},

see Remark
2.7
for the right-hand side.
Moreover if
E
is stable, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{E}\in\mathrm{Im}(j_{b!})\subset\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}_{b})_{w^{\prime}}.

Here
j_{b!}
is the fully-faithful left adjoint of
j_{b}^{*}

for the open immersion

j_{b}\colon\mathcal{H}_{b}^{\mathrm{ss}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{b}

\displaystyle j_{b!}\colon\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}_{b}^{\mathrm{ss}})_{w^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{L}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}_{b})_{w^{\prime}}.

Proof.

We first prove (
5.33
) when
E
is semistable.
Let
\nu
be a map

\displaystyle\nu\colon B\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathcal{H},\ \mathrm{pt}\mapsto M_{1}\oplus M_{2}

for
M_{i}\in\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{C}_{b})
with
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-weights
(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})

such that
\mu_{1}>\mu_{2}
.
Since we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{wt}(\nu^{*}\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{H}}^{\nu>0})=(g-1)(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}),\ \mathrm{wt}(\nu^{*}\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{H}}^{\nu<0})=(g-1)(\mu_{2}-\mu_{1}),

it is enough to show that

(5.34)

\displaystyle\mathrm{wt}(\nu^{*}\mathcal{V}_{E})\subset(g-1)(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\cdot[-1,1]+\frac{w^{\prime}}{2}(\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}).

We may assume that
M_{i}
is supported on
C_{i}
.
Let
\mathcal{L}
and
n_{i}
be taken for
E\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{C}_{b})
as in Lemma
5.2
,
and set
l_{i}:=\deg(\mathcal{L}|_{C_{i}})
.
Then we have

(5.35)

\displaystyle l_{1}+l_{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}n_{i}=w^{\prime}.

By the definition of
\mathcal{V}_{E}
, a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-weight
\mu
of

\nu^{*}{\mathcal{V}_{E}}
is of the form

(5.36)

\displaystyle\mu=l_{1}\mu_{1}+l_{2}\mu_{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}a_{i}\mu_{1}+(n_{i}-a_{i})\mu_{2}

for
0\leqslant a_{i}\leqslant n_{i}
. Here the first terms
l_{1}\mu_{1}+l_{2}\mu_{2}
is the

\mathbb{G}_{m}
-weight of
\nu^{*}\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{L}}
, which is computed from
(
2.27
).
By using (
5.35
), we can write (
5.36
) as

\displaystyle\mu-\frac{w^{\prime}}{2}(\mu_{1}+\mu_{2})=\left(l_{1}+\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}a_{i}-\frac{w^{\prime}}{2}\right)(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}).

We have the exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\operatorname{Ker}(\phi)\to E\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi}}{{\to}}(E|_{C_{2}})^{\mathrm{fr}}\to 0,

where
(-)^{\mathrm{fr}}
is the torsion-free quotient.
Using the exact sequence in Lemma
5.2
, the above exact sequence
is identified with

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\to E\to\mathcal{L}|_{C_{2}}\to 0,

where

\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
is a line bundle on
C_{1}
with
degree
l_{1}-2g+2+\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}n_{i}
.
By the semistability of
E
, we have

(5.37)

\displaystyle l_{1}-2g+2+\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}n_{i}\leqslant l_{2}.

By substituting (
5.35
), the above inequality is equivalent to

\displaystyle l_{1}\leqslant g-1-\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}n_{i}+\frac{w^{\prime}}{2}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\mu-\frac{w^{\prime}}{2}(\mu_{1}+\mu_{2})\leqslant(g-1)(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}).

The lower bound

(5.38)

\displaystyle(g-1)(\mu_{2}-\mu_{1})\leqslant\mu-\frac{w^{\prime}}{2}(\mu_{1}+\mu_{2})

is similarly obtained,
using the exact sequence

(5.39)

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}\to E\to\mathcal{L}|_{C_{1}}\to 0

where
\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}
is a line bundle on
C_{2}
of degree
l_{2}-2g+2+\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}n_{i}
.
Therefore (
5.34
) is proved.

Suppose that
E
is stable. The existence of
j_{!}
and the characterization of its
image for the open immersion
j\colon\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}(w)

is given in Theorem
2.14
and Remark
2.15
, and the same
arguments apply after the base change for
b\in\mathrm{B}
.
If
E
is stable, the inequality (
5.37
) is strict,
hence we obtain the inclusion (
5.34
) without boundary.
Therefore we obtain the second statement, see Remark
2.15
.
∎

We obtain the following corollary, which gives a connection of the Arinkin’s construction of Cohen–Macaulay extension and the limit category:

Corollary 5.10
.

If
E
is semistable, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{E}\in\widetilde{\operatorname{L}}(\mathcal{H}_{b})_{w^{\prime}}.

If
E
is furthermore stable, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{E}\in\operatorname{Im}(j_{b!})\subset\operatorname{L}(\mathcal{H}_{b})_{w^{\prime}}.

Proof.

The corollary follows from Corollary
5.8
and Proposition
5.9
.
∎

5.3.
Preservation of quasi-BPS categories

Let

\displaystyle\operatorname{L}(\mathcal{H}(\chi)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{w}\subset\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}(\chi)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{w}

be the quasi-BPS category over
\mathcal{B}
. Using the results in the previous subsections,
we prove the following:

Proposition 5.11
.

The functor
\Phi
in (
2.31
)
restricts to the functor

\displaystyle\Phi\colon\operatorname{L}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}}\to j_{!}\operatorname{L}(\mathcal{H}(\chi)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{w^{\prime}}.

Here
j\colon\mathcal{H}(\chi)^{\mathrm{ss}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}(\chi)

is the open immersion.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.18
, we may assume that
w<0
.
The proof is long, so we divide it into 6 steps.

Step 1
.

Reduction to an étale neighborhood of each point in the Hitchin base.

We take
b\in\mathcal{B}
such that the spectral curve

\mathcal{C}_{b}
is reducible

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{b}=C_{1}\cup C_{2},\ C_{1}\neq C_{2}.

We take an étale neighborhood

(T,0)\to(\mathcal{B},b)
and set

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{T}:=\mathcal{C}\times_{\mathcal{B}}T,\ \mathcal{H}_{T}(w):=\mathcal{H}(w)\times_{\mathcal{B}}T.

For
t\in T
such that
\mathcal{C}_{t}
is reducible,
we write
\mathcal{C}_{t}=C_{t1}\cup C_{t2}
where
C_{ti}
is deformation equivalent to

C_{i}\subset\mathcal{C}_{b}
.
Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{T}\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}\times_{T}\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi))

be the pull-back of the Arinkin sheaf (
2.28
)
by the projection to
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ss}}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}
.
The Fourier-Mukai functor with kernel
\mathcal{P}_{T}
gives the functor

\displaystyle\Phi_{T}\colon\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}}\to\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi))_{w^{\prime}}.

For
A\in\operatorname{L}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}}
, let
A_{T}\in\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}}
be its pull-back
by
\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}\to\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}
.
It is enough to show that

\displaystyle\Phi_{T}(A_{T})\in j_{T!}\operatorname{L}(\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{w^{\prime}}

for the open immersion
j_{T}\colon\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi)^{\mathrm{ss}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi)
.

Let
\nu
be a map

(5.40)

\displaystyle\nu\colon B\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi),\ \mathrm{pt}\mapsto M=M_{1}\oplus M_{2}

with
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-weight
(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})
,
\mu_{1}>\mu_{2}
,
such that
M_{i}
is a line bundle on
C_{ti}
for
t\in T

satisfying

\displaystyle\chi_{i}=\deg M_{i},\ \chi_{1}+\chi_{2}=\chi,\ \chi_{1}>\chi_{2}.

Namely it corresponds to the center of a Harder-Narasimhan stratum of

\mathcal{H}(\chi)
, see Remark
2.15
.
We need to show the following weight bound

(5.41)

\displaystyle\mathrm{wt}(\iota_{*}\nu^{\mathrm{reg}*}\Phi_{T}(A_{T}))\subset(g-1)(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\cdot(-1,1]+\frac{w^{\prime}}{2}(\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}),

in particular the set of weights in the left-hand side does not attain the left boundary weight.

Step 2
.

Perturbation of stability condition.

Let
h
be an ample divisor on
C
of degree one and we denote by
h_{b}

its pull-back by the projection
\mathcal{C}_{b}\to C
.
We perturb it to a
\mathbb{Q}
-ample divisor
h_{\varepsilon}
on
\mathcal{C}_{b}
such that

(5.42)

\displaystyle\deg h_{\varepsilon}|_{C_{1}}=1-\varepsilon,\ \deg h_{\varepsilon}|_{C_{2}}=1+\varepsilon

for
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{Q}
with
0<\varepsilon\ll 1
.
We can take an étale neighborhood
T\to\mathcal{B}
so that

h_{\varepsilon}
extends to a
\mathbb{Q}
-ample
divisor
h_{T,\varepsilon}
on
\mathcal{C}_{T}
.
We consider open substacks

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}\subset\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}\subset\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)

where
\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}
consists of
h_{T,\varepsilon}
-semistable sheaves on the fibers of
\mathcal{C}_{T}\to T
.
By a choice of
h_{\varepsilon}
, it consists of

h_{T,\varepsilon}
-stable sheaves; otherwise there is
a\in\mathbb{Z}

satisfying
a/(1-\varepsilon)=w/2
, which cannot happen for
0<\varepsilon\ll 1
.
Therefore the morphism to the good moduli space

(5.43)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}\to\mathrm{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}

is a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-gerbe and
\mathrm{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}
is a smooth variety.
Moreover we have an equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}}\simeq\operatorname{Coh}(\mathrm{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}},\beta^{-\chi^{\prime}})

where
\beta
is the Brauer class corresponding to the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-gerbe (
5.43
).

The complement

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}=\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}

is a
\Theta
-stratum
of
\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}
, see
[
22
]
for the notation of

\Theta
-stratum. It consists of

E\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{C}_{t})
for
t\in T

which admits an exact sequence

(5.44)

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{L}_{2}\to E\to\mathcal{L}_{1}\to 0

where
\mathcal{L}_{i}
is a line bundle on
C_{ti}
with
degree
w/2
(so
\mathcal{S}=\emptyset
if
w
is odd). Note that by the condition (
5.42
)
and
w<0
, we have

(5.45)

\displaystyle\frac{w/2}{h_{T,\varepsilon}\cdot C_{t2}}>\frac{w/2}{h_{T,\varepsilon}\cdot C_{t1}}.

This implies that the exact sequence (
5.44
) is a Harder-Narasimhan filtration of
E

with respect to
h_{T,\varepsilon}
.

Let
g\colon\mathcal{S}\to\mathcal{Z}
be the center of
\mathcal{S}
.
It corresponds to direct sums
\mathcal{L}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{L}_{2}
,
hence we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}^{\mathrm{cl}}\cong(\mathrm{Pic}^{w/2}(C)\times B\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\times 2}.

Moreover the stack
\mathcal{S}
is quasi-smooth, the closed immersion

\mathcal{S}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}
is quasi-smooth
and the natural map

g\colon\mathcal{S}\to\mathcal{Z}

is a vector bundle with fiber

(5.46)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ext}_{S}^{1}(\mathcal{L}_{2},\mathcal{L}_{1})=\mathbb{A}^{2g-2}.

These follow from the computations of tangent complexes

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{S}/\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}}|_{(0\to\mathcal{L}_{2}\to E\to\mathcal{L}_{1}\to 0)}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Hom}_{S}(\mathcal{L}_{2},\mathcal{L}_{1})

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{S}/\mathcal{Z}}|_{(0\to\mathcal{L}_{2}\to E\to\mathcal{L}_{1}\to 0)}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Hom}_{S}(\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2})[1]

and noting that

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{S}(\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2})=k^{2g-2}[-1],\ \operatorname{Hom}_{S}(\mathcal{L}_{2},\mathcal{L}_{1})=k^{2g-2}[-1].

For a point
z\in\mathcal{Z}(k)
corresponding to the
direct sum
\mathcal{L}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{L}_{2}
, there is a canonical
map
B\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathcal{Z}
with image
z
, and the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-weight
is given by

(5.47)

\displaystyle\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}),\ \lambda_{1}<\lambda_{2}

(so that (
5.46
) has positive
\lambda
-weights and
g_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}
has non-negative
\lambda
-weights).
Since
\mathcal{Z}
is connected,
\lambda
is independent of
z
.
We have the semiorthogonal decomposition

\displaystyle\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{S})=\left\langle\ldots,\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{S})_{-1},\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{S})_{0},\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{S})_{1},\ldots\right\rangle

with an equivalence
g^{*}\colon\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{Z})_{a}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{S})_{a}
, where

\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{Z})_{a}\subset\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{Z})
corresponds to the

\lambda
-weight
a
-part, see
[
21
, Proposition 1.1.2, Lemma 1.5.6]
.

Step 3
.

Reduction to the case of perturbed stability condition.

For an object
A_{T}
as in Step
1
, by the definition of
\operatorname{L}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}}
, we have

(5.48)

\displaystyle A_{T}|_{\mathcal{S}}\in\left\langle\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{S})_{a}:a\in I\right\rangle

where
I
is the interval

\displaystyle I=(g-1)(\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1})\cdot[-1,1]-\frac{\chi^{\prime}}{2}(\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}).

We consider the following diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}\times_{T}\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}\times_{T}\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{T}}
\scriptstyle{q_{T}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi).}

We set

(5.49)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{T}^{\varepsilon}:=\gamma^{*}\mathcal{P}_{T}\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}\times_{T}\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi))

and
consider the Fourier-Mukai functor with kernel
\mathcal{P}_{T}^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\Phi_{T}^{\varepsilon}\colon\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}}\to\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi))_{w^{\prime}}.

Then we have the distinguished triangle

(5.50)

\displaystyle p_{T*}(\Gamma_{q_{T}^{-1}(\mathcal{S})}(q_{T}^{*}A_{T}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{T}))\to\Phi_{T}(A_{T})\to\Phi_{T}^{\varepsilon}(A_{T}^{\varepsilon})

where
A_{T}^{\varepsilon}:=\gamma^{*}A_{T}
, see (
1.23
) for the local cohomology functor.
For the map
\nu
in (
5.40
), we have

(5.51)

\displaystyle\nu^{*}p_{T*}(\Gamma_{q_{T}^{-1}(\mathcal{S})}(q_{T}^{*}A_{T}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{T}))\cong\Gamma(\mathcal{H}_{t}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}},\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}_{t}}(A_{t}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{M})).

In the next step, using the condition (
5.48
),
we show that the above object vanishes.
Therefore in order to show the weight bound (
5.41
),
it is enough to show the same condition for
\Phi_{T}^{\varepsilon}(A_{T}^{\varepsilon})
,

(5.52)

\displaystyle\mathrm{wt}(\iota_{*}\nu^{\mathrm{reg}*}\Phi_{T}^{\varepsilon}(A_{T}^{\varepsilon}))\subset(g-1)(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\cdot(-1,1]+\frac{w^{\prime}}{2}(\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}).

Step 4
.

The object (
5.51
) is zero.

We show that (
5.51
) vanishes.
Note that the object
M=M_{1}\oplus M_{2}
in (
5.40
) fits into
an exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\to M\to\mathcal{O}_{C_{t1}\cap C_{t2}}\to 0.

Here
the line bundle
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}
on
\mathcal{C}_{t}
satisfies
\deg(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}|_{C_{ti}})=\chi_{i}
.
Therefore by Corollary
5.8
, it is enough to show that

(5.53)

\displaystyle\Gamma(\mathcal{H}_{t}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}},\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}_{t}}(A_{t}\otimes\mathcal{V}_{M}))=0

where
\mathcal{V}_{M}
is a vector bundle of the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{M}=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{\ell}\mathcal{F}_{c_{i}}^{\otimes m_{i}}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}}\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{H}_{t}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}).

Here
c_{i}\in C
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant\ell
and
m_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
satisfy

\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}m_{i}=2g-2
.

The local cohomology functor is calculated by

(5.54)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}_{t}}(A_{t}\otimes\mathcal{V}_{M})=\operatorname*{colim}_{\mathcal{S}_{t}\subset\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{H}_{t}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}},A_{t}\otimes\mathcal{V}_{M}).

Here the colimit is taken for closed substacks
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{H}_{t}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}
with
\mathrm{Supp}(\mathcal{S}^{\prime})=\mathrm{Supp}(\mathcal{S}_{t})
.
We have

(5.55)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{H}_{t}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{t}},A_{t}\otimes\mathcal{V}_{M})=(A_{t}\otimes\mathcal{V}_{M})|_{\mathcal{S}_{t}}\otimes\det\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{S}_{t}/\mathcal{H}_{t}}[-d].

Here
d
is the codimension
\mathcal{S}_{t}
in
\mathcal{H}_{t}
.
We show that it has negative
\lambda
-weights, where
\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})

with
\lambda_{1}<\lambda_{2}
is
given by (
5.47
). Indeed,

\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{S}_{t}/\mathcal{H}_{t}}[-1]
is a vector bundle on
\mathcal{S}_{t}
of rank
2g-2
with
\lambda
-weights
(\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1})>0
.
Therefore

\det\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{S}_{t}/\mathcal{H}_{t}}
has
\lambda
-weight

(2g-2)(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})<0
.
It follows that, by
the condition (
5.48
), a
\lambda
-weight of (
5.55
) is
at most

\displaystyle(g-1)(\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1})-\frac{\chi^{\prime}}{2}(\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})+\chi_{1}\lambda_{1}+\chi_{2}\lambda_{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}(a_{i}\lambda_{1}+(m_{i}-a_{i})\lambda_{2})+(2g-2)(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})

\displaystyle=\left(\chi_{1}-\frac{\chi}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}a_{i}\right)(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})<0

since
\chi_{1}>\chi_{2}
with
\chi_{1}+\chi_{2}=\chi
and
a_{i}\geqslant 0
.
Therefore (
5.55
) has negative
\lambda
-weights.

There is a sequence of closed immersions

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{t}=\mathcal{S}_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{S}_{1}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{S}_{2}\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow

where each
\mathcal{S}_{n}
is a
n
-th infinitesimal thickening of
\mathcal{S}_{t}
in
\mathcal{H}_{t}
,
whose structure sheaves fit into distinguished triangles

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sym}^{n}(\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{S}_{t}/\mathcal{H}_{t}}[-1])\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{n+1}}\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{n}}.

Then
\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{H}_{t}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{n}},A_{t}\otimes\mathcal{V}_{M})
is filtered by

(5.56)

\displaystyle(A_{t}\otimes\mathcal{V}_{M})|_{\mathcal{S}_{t}}\otimes\det\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{S}_{t}/\mathcal{H}_{t}}[-d]\otimes\mathrm{Sym}^{i}(\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{S}_{t}/\mathcal{H}_{t}}[-1])^{\vee}

for
0\leqslant i\leqslant n-1
. Since the first two factors and the last factor have negative
\lambda
-weights,
we conclude that (
5.56
) has negative

\lambda
-weights.

Now for the good moduli space map

g_{t}\colon\mathcal{H}_{t}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}\to\mathrm{H}_{t}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}
, we have
the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{H}_{t}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r_{t}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}_{t}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{S}_{t}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{t}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}_{t}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z_{t}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Here the top and the right bottom horizontal arrows are good moduli space morphisms.
Since (
5.56
) has negative
\lambda
-weights,
its push-forward
g_{t*}
has also negative
\lambda
-weights, therefore
its push-forward to
Z_{t}
is zero.
Therefore from the above commutative diagram, we have

(5.57)

\displaystyle r_{t*}\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{H}_{t}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{n}},A_{t}\otimes\mathcal{V}_{M})=0.

Note that we have

\displaystyle\operatorname*{colim}_{\mathcal{S}_{t}\subset\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{H}_{t}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}},A_{t}\otimes\mathcal{V}_{M})=\operatorname*{colim}_{n\geqslant 0}\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{H}_{t}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{n}},A_{t}\otimes\mathcal{V}_{M}).

Since
r_{t*}
is continuous, by (
5.54
) and (
5.57
) we obtain the vanishing

\displaystyle r_{t*}\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}_{t}}(A_{t}\otimes\mathcal{V}_{M})=0.

Therefore (
5.53
) holds by taking the global section over
\mathrm{H}_{t}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}
.

Step 5
.

Proof of (
5.52
).

It remains to prove that (
5.52
) holds.
For a closed point
y\in\mathrm{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}
which
lies over
t\in T
,
let
\mathcal{O}_{y}
be the corresponding object

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{y}\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathrm{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}},\beta^{-\chi^{\prime}})\simeq\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}}.

Then we show that

(5.58)

\displaystyle\mathrm{wt}(\iota_{*}\nu^{\mathrm{reg}*}\Phi_{T}^{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{O}_{y}))\subset(g-1)(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\cdot(-1,1]+\frac{w^{\prime}}{2}(\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}).

Indeed let
E_{y}\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{C}_{t})
be the corresponding
h_{\varepsilon}
-stable sheaf.
In the proof of Proposition
5.9
, the lower bound (
5.38
)
of
\mathcal{V}_{E_{y}}

is achieved only if there is an exact sequence as in (
5.39
)

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{L}_{2}\to E_{y}\to\mathcal{L}_{1}\to 0

where
\mathcal{L}_{i}
is a line bundle on

C_{ti}
with
\deg\mathcal{L}_{i}=w/2<0
.
By a choice of
h_{\varepsilon}
satisfying (
5.42
), it violates the
h_{\varepsilon}
-stability of
E_{y}
, because of the inequality (
5.45
).
Therefore (
5.58
) holds.

The property (
5.58
) implies the following, see Remark
2.15

(5.59)

\displaystyle\Phi_{T}^{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{O}_{y})\in\mathrm{Im}(j_{T!})\subset\operatorname{L}(\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi))_{w^{\prime}}.

Now the image of

(5.60)

\displaystyle(\operatorname{id}\times j_{T})_{!}\colon\operatorname{L}(\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}

\displaystyle\times_{T}\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{(-\chi^{\prime},w^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\operatorname{L}(\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}\times_{T}\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi))_{(-\chi^{\prime},w^{\prime})}

is a semiorthogonal summand

\displaystyle\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}\times_{T}\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi))=\langle\ldots,\mathrm{Im}(\operatorname{id}\times j_{T})_{!},\ldots\rangle

by Lemma
6.2
(more precisely by its
\beta^{-\chi^{\prime}}
-twisted version for
T^{\prime}=\mathrm{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}
, the good moduli
space
\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}\to\mathrm{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}
of the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-gerbe
(
5.43
)).
Together with (
5.59
), this implies that the kernel object

\mathcal{P}_{T}^{\varepsilon}
of
\Phi_{T}^{\varepsilon}
in (
5.49
) lies
in the image of (
5.60
) because the semiorthogonal factors of
\mathcal{P}^{\varepsilon}_{T}
for
the other components are zero, see the argument of
[
32
, Lemma 6.2]
.
In particular
the image of
\Phi_{T}^{\varepsilon}
lies in

\mathrm{Im}(j_{T!})
.
Therefore (
5.52
) holds.

Step 6
.

Conclusion of the proof of the proposition.

By the arguments as above,
for a map
\nu
as in (
5.40
) we have obtained the required weight bound. For a map
\nu
with opposite ordering
\chi_{2}>\chi_{1}
and
\mu_{2}>\mu_{1}
, we also obtain the
required weight bound by the same argument, by replacing
h_{\varepsilon}
with

h_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle\deg h^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}|_{C_{1}}=1+\varepsilon,\ \deg h^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}|_{C_{2}}=1-\varepsilon.

Therefore the proposition holds.
∎

5.4.
Proof of Whittaker normalization for
\operatorname{GL}_{2}

Recall the functor (
2.31
) induced by the Arinkin sheaf

\displaystyle\Phi\colon\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{-\chi^{\prime}}\to\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}(\chi))_{w^{\prime}}.

Also recall the Hitchin section
s
and its
!
-push forward in (
4.7
).
We show that Conjecture
4.4
is satisfied in this case:

Proposition 5.12
.

For
G=\operatorname{GL}_{2}
, there is an isomorphism

\displaystyle\Phi(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}})\cong s_{!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}}.

Proof.

Recall in Subsection
4.2
that the Hitchin section
s
factors through

\displaystyle s\colon\mathcal{B}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\overline{s}}}{{\to}}\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{ss}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle j}}{{\hookrightarrow}}\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0})

for the quasi-smooth closed immersion
\overline{s}
.
By Proposition
5.11
,
it is enough to show that

\displaystyle\Phi(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}})|_{\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{ss}}}\cong\overline{s}_{!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}}.

By Lemma
4.2
, we have

\displaystyle\overline{s}^{*}(\Phi(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}})|_{\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{ss}}})\cong h_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}}

where
h\colon\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{ss}}\to\mathcal{B}
is the Hitchin map.
By the adjunction, we have the map

(5.61)

\displaystyle\overline{s}_{!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}}\to\Phi(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}})|_{\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{ss}}}.

In what follows, we show that (
5.61
) is an isomorphism.

We use the notation in the proof of Proposition
5.11
.
By applying (
5.50
) to
A=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}}
,
we obtain the distinguished triangle

\displaystyle p_{T*}\Gamma_{q_{T}^{-1}(\mathcal{S})}(\mathcal{P}_{T})\to\Phi_{T}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}})\to\Phi_{T}^{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}}).

We take a map

\displaystyle\nu\colon\operatorname{Spec}k\to\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi_{0})^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}

and let
M\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{C}_{t})
be the corresponding
h_{\varepsilon}
-semistable sheaf for
t\in T
.
In Lemma
5.13
below, we show that

(5.62)

\displaystyle\nu^{*}p_{T*}\Gamma_{q_{T}^{-1}(\mathcal{S})}(\mathcal{P}_{T})=\Gamma(\mathcal{H}_{t}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}},\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}_{t}}(\mathcal{P}_{M}))=0.

It follows that we have the isomorphism

(5.63)

\displaystyle\Phi_{T}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}})|_{\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi_{0})^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}\Phi_{T}^{\varepsilon}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}})|_{\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi_{0})^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}}.

Since the

h_{\varepsilon}
-semistability is equivalent to
the
h_{\varepsilon}
-stability,
the Fourier-Mukai functor

(5.64)

\displaystyle\Phi_{T}^{\varepsilon,\varepsilon}\colon\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}})_{-\chi_{0}^{\prime}}\to\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi_{0})^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}})_{w^{\prime}}

given by the kernel sheaf

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{T}^{\varepsilon,\varepsilon}:=\mathcal{P}_{T}|_{\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}\times_{T}\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi_{0})^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}}\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}\times_{T}\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi_{0})^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}})

is an equivalence by Theorem
2.19
(more precisely its obvious generalization to the case of families over
T
), also see Remark
2.20
.
The kernel object of its inverse
functor
(\Phi_{T}^{\varepsilon,\varepsilon})^{-1}
is given by

(5.65)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{P}_{T}^{\varepsilon,\varepsilon})^{\vee}[4g-3]\in\operatorname{Coh}^{\heartsuit}(\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}\times_{T}\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi_{0})^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}).

The base-change of the Hitchin section
\overline{s}
to
T
, denoted
\overline{s}_{T}
, further factors through

\displaystyle\overline{s}_{T}\colon T\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\overline{s}_{T}^{\varepsilon}}}{{\hookrightarrow}}\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi_{0})^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}\subset\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{ss}}

for the closed immersion
\overline{s}_{T}^{\varepsilon}
.
Then Lemma
4.2
together with the description of the kernel object (
5.65
) of
(\Phi_{T}^{\varepsilon,\varepsilon})^{-1}
imply that

\displaystyle(\Phi_{T}^{\varepsilon,\varepsilon})^{-1}(\overline{s}_{T!}^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}_{T})\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}}.

By the equivalence (
5.64
), it follows that

\displaystyle\Phi_{T}^{\varepsilon,\varepsilon}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}})\cong\overline{s}_{T!}^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{O}_{T}.

Together with (
5.63
), we obtain the isomorphism

\displaystyle\Phi_{T}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}})|_{\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi_{0})^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}\overline{s}_{T!}\mathcal{O}_{T}|_{\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi_{0})^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}}.

By replacing the
h_{\varepsilon}
-stability with the
h_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}
-stability in the proof of Proposition
5.11
Step
6
,
we also obtain

\displaystyle\Phi_{T}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}})|_{\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi_{0})^{\varepsilon^{\prime}\text{-ss}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}\overline{s}_{T!}\mathcal{O}_{T}|_{\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi_{0})^{\varepsilon^{\prime}\text{-ss}}}.

Note that we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{ss}}=\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi_{0})^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}\cup\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi_{0})^{\varepsilon^{\prime}\text{-ss}}\cup\mathcal{Z}

where
\mathcal{Z}
is a closed substack of
\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{ss}}

corresponding to strictly polystable sheaves (which also appears as a center of
\mathcal{S}
, see the proof of Proposition
5.11
).
Since the image of
\overline{s}_{T}
is a closed substack of
\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{ss}}
disjoint from
\mathcal{Z}
,
we have

(5.66)

\displaystyle\Phi_{T}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}_{T}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}})\cong s_{T!}\mathcal{O}_{T}\oplus R

where
R
is supported on
\mathcal{Z}
.

Now we show that
the restriction functor

\displaystyle\operatorname{L}(\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{ss}})_{w^{\prime}}\to\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{ss}}\setminus\mathcal{Z})

is conservative. We have the following diagram

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle q}}{{\leftarrow}}\mathcal{S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\hookrightarrow}}\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{ss}}

where
\mathcal{S}
is the
\Theta
-stratum with support
the complement of
\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0})^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}
.
Then as in the proof of
[
34
, Theorem 7.18]
, we have the semiorthogonal decomposition

\displaystyle\operatorname{L}(\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{ss}})_{w^{\prime}}=\langle p_{*}q^{*}\operatorname{L}(\mathcal{Z})_{\delta},\mathcal{W}\rangle,\ \mathcal{W}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\operatorname{L}(\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0})^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}})_{w^{\prime}}

for some
\delta
. Then if
A\in\operatorname{L}(\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{ss}})_{w^{\prime}}

satisfies
A|_{\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{ss}}\setminus\mathcal{Z}}=0
,
then
A|_{\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0})^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}}=0
as
\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0})^{\varepsilon\text{-ss}}\subset\mathcal{H}(\chi_{0})^{\mathrm{ss}}\setminus\mathcal{Z}
. Then from the above semiorthogonal decomposition,
A=p_{*}q^{*}A^{\prime}
for some
A^{\prime}\in\operatorname{L}(\mathcal{Z})_{\delta}
.
Then
q^{*}A^{\prime}
is zero on
\mathcal{S}\setminus\mathcal{Z}
, and
\mathcal{S}\to\mathcal{Z}
is a vector bundle, therefore
A^{\prime}=0
.

It follows that (
5.61
) is an isomorphism for any étale neighborhood of

b\in\mathcal{B}
, therefore it is an isomorphism.
∎

Lemma 5.13
.

We have the vanishing (
5.62
).

Proof.

The proof follows as in Step
4
of Proposition
5.11
,
and we use the notation in loc. cit.
Indeed, it is enough to prove

\displaystyle\Gamma(\mathcal{H}_{t}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}},\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}_{t}}(\mathcal{V}_{M}))=0.

Let

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{L}\to M\to Q\to 0

be an exact sequence as in Lemma
5.2
, where

\mathcal{L}
is a line bundle on
\mathcal{C}_{t}
and
Q=\oplus_{i=1}^{\ell}Q_{i}
with
\chi(Q_{i})=n_{i}
. Let
l_{i}=\deg(\mathcal{L}|_{C_{ti}})
.
Note that
l_{1}+l_{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}n_{i}=0
since
\deg\pi_{*}M=\chi_{0}=2-2g
.
Then as in the proof of Step
4
of Proposition
5.11
, the
\lambda
-weights of
\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{H}_{t}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{n}},\mathcal{V}_{M})

are bounded by

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}(a_{i}\lambda_{1}+(n_{i}-a_{i})\lambda_{2})+l_{1}\lambda_{1}+l_{2}\lambda_{2}+(2g-2)(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})

(5.67)

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}a_{i}+l_{1}+2g-2\right)(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}).

We have the exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\to M\to\mathcal{L}|_{C_{t1}}\to 0

where
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
is a line bundle on
C_{t2}
. By the
h_{\varepsilon}
-stability of
M
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{l_{1}}{h_{T,\varepsilon}\cdot C_{t1}}=\frac{l_{1}}{1-\varepsilon}>\frac{1}{2}\deg\pi_{*}M=1-g.

Together with
\lambda_{1}<\lambda_{2}
, it follows that
(
5.67
) is negative, therefore
\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{H}_{t}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{n}},\mathcal{V}_{M})

has negative
\lambda
-weights. Then the vanishing (
5.62
) holds
as in the proof of Step
4
of Proposition
5.11
.
∎

6.
Proofs of some lemmas

6.1.
Proof of Lemma
3.1

Proof.

We
show that
\mathrm{ev}_{1}
,
(\mathrm{ev}_{3},\mathrm{ev}_{2})
are quasi-smooth and proper
of relative virtual dimensions
1,-1
respectively; the other statements
are also proved similarly.
In general for a diagram of smooth stacks

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\leftarrow}}\mathcal{Z}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\to}}\mathcal{Y}

we have the following diagram

\textstyle{\Omega_{(f,g)}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\textstyle{g^{*}\Omega_{\mathcal{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega_{\mathcal{Y}}}
\textstyle{\Omega_{\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces f^{*}\Omega_{\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega_{\mathcal{Z}}.}

Suppose that
f
is smooth and
g
is smooth and proper. Then all the arrows in the
middle squares are quasi-smooth closed immersions. Therefore, in the diagram

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathcal{X}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f^{\prime}}}{{\leftarrow}}\Omega_{(f,g)}[-1]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g^{\prime}}}{{\to}}\Omega_{\mathcal{Y}}

the map
f^{\prime}
is quasi-smooth and
g^{\prime}
is proper.

The diagram (
3.2
)
is obtained from the above construction from the left Hecke diagram of moduli stacks of
bundles, see
[
34
, Section 9.4]
.
Namely let
\mathrm{Hecke}_{G}^{\mathrm{B}}
be the moduli stack which classifies exact
sequences

\displaystyle 0\to F_{1}\to F_{2}\to\mathcal{O}_{x}\to 0

where
F_{i}
are vector bundles on
C
of rank
r

and
x\in C
. We have the evaluation morphisms

(6.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}(1)\times\mathrm{Bun}_{G}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\mathrm{B}},\mathrm{ev}_{2}^{\mathrm{B}})}}{{\leftarrow}}\mathrm{Hecke}_{G}^{\mathrm{B}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{\mathrm{B}}}}{{\to}}\mathrm{Bun}_{G}.

Here
\mathcal{C}(1)=C\times B\mathbb{G}_{m}
is the moduli stack of skyscraper sheaves
\mathcal{O}_{x}
for
x\in C
.
Then as in the proof of
[
34
, Lemma 9.4]
, the resulting diagram

(6.2)

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathcal{C}(1)}\times\Omega_{\mathrm{Bun}_{G}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\mathrm{B}},\mathrm{ev}_{2}^{\mathrm{B}})^{\prime}}}{{\leftarrow}}\Omega_{(\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\mathrm{B}},\mathrm{ev}_{2}^{\mathrm{B}},\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{\mathrm{B}})}[-1]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{\mathrm{B}})^{\prime}}}{{\to}}\Omega_{\mathrm{Bun}_{G}}

is identified with the diagram (
3.2
).
Therefore
\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{\sharp}
is proper and
(\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\sharp},\mathrm{ev}_{2}^{\sharp})
is quasi-smooth. By the base change, the map
(\mathrm{ev}_{3},\mathrm{ev}_{2})
is quasi-smooth. Also as
S\hookrightarrow S\times k[-1]
is a closed
immersion, the map
\mathrm{ev}_{1}
is proper.

In a similar way, the diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{Hecke}_{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{\sharp},\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\sharp})}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ev}_{2}^{\sharp}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}\times\mathfrak{M}(1)}

is obtained by the above construction from the right Hecke diagram, see
[
34
, Subsection 9.3]
. Therefore
\mathrm{ev}_{2}^{\sharp}
is proper and
(\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{\sharp},\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\sharp})
is quasi-smooth.
By the same argument as above, the map
(\mathrm{ev}_{1},\mathrm{ev}_{3})
is quasi-smooth and
\mathrm{ev}_{2}
is proper.

Since
S\times\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}\to\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}
is smooth,
we conclude that
\mathrm{ev}_{1}
is quasi-smooth.
It remains to show that
(\mathrm{ev}_{3},\mathrm{ev}_{2})
is proper.
By the same argument as above, we know that

\mathrm{ev}_{2}
is proper and quasi-smooth.
The map
(\mathrm{ev}_{3},\mathrm{ev}_{2})
factors through

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hecke}^{\prime}_{G}\to\mathrm{C}\times_{\mathrm{B}}\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}\hookrightarrow S\times\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}.

Since
\mathrm{C}\to\mathrm{B}
is proper, the
morphism

\mathrm{C}\times_{\mathrm{B}}\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}\to\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}
is also proper, therefore
we conclude that
(\mathrm{ev}_{3},\mathrm{ev}_{2})
is proper.

The map
\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{\sharp}
in (
3.2
) has relative virtual dimension zero,
since the
\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{\sharp}
-relative cotangent complex at (
3.3
) is
\operatorname{Hom}^{*}_{S}(E_{1},\mathcal{O}_{x})
whose Euler characteristic is zero.
By the base change (
3.8
), we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{vdim}\mathrm{Hecke}_{G}^{\prime}=\operatorname{vdim}\mathrm{Hecke}_{G}+1,

therefore
