we conclude that the map
\mathrm{ev}_{1}
has relative virtual dimension one.
In a similar way, the map
\mathrm{ev}_{2}
has relative virtual dimension one,
hence
(\mathrm{ev}_{3},\mathrm{ev}_{2})
,
(\mathrm{ev}_{3},\mathrm{ev}_{1})
have virtual dimension
-1
.
∎

6.2.
Proof of Lemma
3.2

Proof.

In
[
34
, Section 9.4]
, it is shown that the diagram (
6.1
) induces the functor

\displaystyle(\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{\mathrm{B}})_{*}^{\Omega}(\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\mathrm{B}},\mathrm{ev}_{2}^{\mathrm{B}})^{\Omega!}\colon\operatorname{L}(\mathfrak{M}(1))_{0}\otimes

\displaystyle\operatorname{L}(\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}(\chi))_{\delta\otimes\omega_{\mathrm{Bun}_{G}}^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\to\operatorname{L}(\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}(\chi-1))_{\delta\otimes\omega_{\mathrm{Bun}_{G}}^{1/2}}.

Here we refer to loc. cit. for the notation
(-)_{*}^{\Omega}
and
(-)^{\Omega!}
.
Unraveling their definitions and identifications of the diagram (
6.1
) with (
6.2
),
the above functor is identified with

(6.7)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{\mathrm{B}})_{*}^{\Omega}(\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\mathrm{B}},\mathrm{ev}_{2}^{\mathrm{B}})^{\Omega!}=(\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{\sharp})_{*}((\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\sharp},\mathrm{ev}_{2}^{\sharp})^{*}\otimes\omega_{(\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\mathrm{B}},\mathrm{ev}_{2}^{\mathrm{B}})}[d])

where
d
is the relative virtual dimension of
(\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\mathrm{B}},\mathrm{ev}_{2}^{\mathrm{B}})
. Note that
\omega_{\mathrm{Bun}_{G}}^{1/2}

exists as a line bundle on
\mathrm{Bun}_{G}
once we fix
\omega_{C}^{1/2}\in\mathrm{Pic}(C)
,
see
[
7
, Section 4]
. Also note that

\displaystyle\operatorname{L}(\mathfrak{M}(1))_{0}=\operatorname{Coh}(S\times k[-1]\times B\mathbb{G}_{m})_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}\operatorname{Coh}(S\times k[-1]).

We consider the functor

(6.8)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Coh}(S\times k[-1])\otimes\operatorname{L}(\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}(\chi))_{w}\to\operatorname{L}(\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}(\chi-1))_{w}

given by

\displaystyle(-)\mapsto(\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{\mathrm{B}})^{\Omega}_{*}(\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\mathrm{B}},\mathrm{ev}_{2}^{\mathrm{B}})^{\Omega!}((-)\boxtimes\omega_{\mathrm{Bun}_{G}}^{1/2})\otimes\omega_{\mathrm{Bun}_{G}}^{-1/2}.

Then from (
6.7
), the above functor is of the form

\displaystyle(-)\mapsto(\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{\sharp})_{*}((\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\sharp},\mathrm{ev}_{2}^{\sharp})^{*}(-)\otimes\mathcal{L}[d])

where
\mathcal{L}
is a line bundle on
\mathrm{Hecke}_{G}
of the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}=\omega_{(\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\mathrm{B}},\mathrm{ev}_{2}^{\mathrm{B}})}\otimes\omega_{\mathrm{Bun}_{G}(\chi)}^{1/2}\otimes\omega_{\mathrm{Bun}_{G}(\chi-1)}^{-1/2}.

Here we have omitted the relevant pull-backs to
\mathrm{Hecke}_{G}
.
The fibers of each line bundle are given by

\displaystyle\omega_{(\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\mathrm{B}},\mathrm{ev}_{2}^{\mathrm{B}})}|_{(F_{1},\theta_{1})\subset(F_{2},\theta_{2})}=\det\chi_{C}(F_{2},\mathcal{O}_{x})^{\vee},

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathrm{Bun}_{G}(\chi)}^{1/2}|_{(F_{1},\theta_{1})\subset(F_{2},\theta_{2})}=\det(\chi_{C}(F_{2},F_{2})[1])^{-1/2},

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathrm{Bun}_{G}(\chi-1)}^{1/2}|_{(F_{1},\theta_{1})\subset(F_{2},\theta_{2})}=\det(\chi_{C}(F_{1},F_{1})[1])^{-1/2}

Since
[F_{1}]=[F_{2}]-[\mathcal{O}_{x}]
in K-theory, by substituting it to
\chi_{C}(F_{1},F_{1})
,
a simple calculation shows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}|_{(F_{1},\theta_{1})\subset(F_{2},\theta_{2})}

\displaystyle=\det\chi_{C}(F_{2},\mathcal{O}_{x})^{-1/2}\otimes\det\chi_{C}(\mathcal{O}_{x},F_{2})^{1/2}\otimes\det\chi_{C}(\mathcal{O}_{x},\mathcal{O}_{x})^{1/2}

\displaystyle\cong\det\chi_{C}(F_{2},\mathcal{O}_{x})^{-1/2}\otimes\det\chi_{C}(F_{2},\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{x})^{1/2}\otimes\det\chi_{C}(\mathcal{O}_{x},\mathcal{O}_{x})^{1/2}.

From the above description, the line bundle
\mathcal{L}
is pulled back from a
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}

on
\mathfrak{M}(1)\times\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}(\chi)
, which is trivial on
\Omega_{U\times B\mathbb{G}_{m}}\times\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}(\chi)

for any affine open subset
U\subset C
such that
\omega_{C}|_{U}\cong\mathcal{O}_{U}
. In particular it
satisfies that for any
\nu\colon B\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathfrak{M}(1)\times\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}(\chi)
we have
c_{1}(\nu^{*}\mathcal{L}^{\prime})=0
.

It follows that the twist by
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
does not change the source of (
6.8
),
therefore we obtain the functor

\displaystyle(\mathrm{ev}_{1}^{\sharp})_{*}(\mathrm{ev}_{3}^{\sharp},\mathrm{ev}_{2}^{\sharp})^{*}(-)\colon\operatorname{Coh}(S\times k[-1])\otimes\operatorname{L}(\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}(\chi))_{w}\to\operatorname{L}(\mathrm{Higgs}_{G}(\chi-1))_{w}.

Finally, the functor
\mathrm{H}
is given as the composition of the above functor with

i_{*}\colon\operatorname{Coh}(S)\to\operatorname{Coh}(S\times k[-1])
for the closed immersion
i\colon S\hookrightarrow S\times k[-1]
, therefore we obtain the lemma.
∎

6.3.
Proof of Proposition
3.9

Proof.

We explain how to modify the proof of the commutativity of the diagram (
3.5
). The only point for the modification is to prove a variant of Proposition
3.7
, namely we need to show that

(6.9)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}^{R}(\mathcal{P}_{E})\cong\mathcal{P}_{E}\boxtimes E^{\vee}[1].

Here
E^{\vee}=\mathbb{D}_{S}(E)[1]
.
Let
\psi
be the isomorphism given by the shifted derived dual on
S

\displaystyle\psi\colon\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}},\ E\mapsto E^{\vee}.

Then we have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}ecke^{\mathrm{greg}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathrm{ev}_{2},\mathrm{ev}_{3})}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ev}_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}\times S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi\times\operatorname{id}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}ecke^{\mathrm{greg}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ev}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{(\mathrm{ev}_{1},\mathrm{ev}_{3})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{greg}}\times S.}

Here the middle vertical arrow is given by

\displaystyle(0\to E_{1}\to E_{2}\to\mathcal{O}_{x}\to 0)\mapsto(0\to E_{2}^{\vee}\to E_{1}^{\vee}\to\mathcal{O}_{x}\to 0)

Then it is straightforward to check that (
6.9
) holds
from Proposition
3.7
and the diagram (
6.3
),
noting that

\psi_{*}\mathcal{P}_{E}\cong\mathcal{P}_{E^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathcal{C}_{b}}^{-1}}
.

The isomorphism (
6.9
) implies that the diagram (
3.9
) commutes
at the level of
k
-valued points; the rest of the arguments are
the same as those in the proof of the commutative diagram (
3.5
)
in Subsection
3.6
, namely we compare the kernel
objects of both compositions, show that both are Cohen–Macaulay sheaves
which are isomorphic in codimension one, hence an isomorphism.
Since the argument is the same, we omit details.
∎

6.4.
Proof of Lemma
4.26

Proof.

Let
K
be the total ring of fractions of
R

\displaystyle K=\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}k((t_{i})).

Then for a given embedding
\phi\colon R\hookrightarrow M
,
it induces the isomorphism
\phi_{K}\colon K\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}M\otimes_{R}K
.
By setting
I=\phi_{K}^{-1}(M)
, we have

R\subset I\subset K
.
Let
I^{\mathrm{sat}}:=\widetilde{R}I\subset K
. It is a rank one
torsion-free module of
\widetilde{R}
which contains
\widetilde{R}
,
therefore it is written as

(6.14)

\displaystyle I^{\mathrm{sat}}=\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}t^{-a_{i}}k[[t_{i}]],\ a_{i}\geqslant 0.

It is enough to show that if
\phi\colon R\hookrightarrow M
attains
\delta
,
then
a_{i}=0
.

By (
6.14
), there is
u\in I
such that
\mathrm{val}_{i}(u)=-a_{i}
,
where
\mathrm{val}_{i}(u)
is the valuation of the
i
-th component of
u
.
Then by replacing
\phi
with
\phi^{\prime}\colon R\hookrightarrow M
with
\phi^{\prime}(1)=u\phi(1)
,
we obtain
R\subset I^{\prime}=u^{-1}I\subset K
.
We have the exact sequences

\displaystyle 0\to I/R\to I^{\mathrm{sat}}/R\to I^{\mathrm{sat}}/I\to 0,

\displaystyle 0\to I^{\prime}/R\to\widetilde{R}/R\to\widetilde{R}/I^{\prime}\to 0.

We also have the isomorphism
u\colon\widetilde{R}/I^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}I^{\mathrm{sat}}/I
.
Therefore

\displaystyle\dim I/R

\displaystyle=\dim I^{\mathrm{sat}}/R-\dim\widetilde{R}/R+\dim I^{\prime}/R

\displaystyle=\dim I^{\mathrm{sat}}/\widetilde{R}+\dim I^{\prime}/R

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}a_{i}+\dim I^{\prime}/R.

By the definition of
\delta
, we have
\dim I/R\leqslant\dim I^{\prime}/R
,
therefore
a_{i}=0
for all
i
.
∎

6.5.
Categories of functors

For cocomplete dg-categories
\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathcal{C}_{2}
, let

\mathrm{Funct}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathcal{C}_{2})
be the dg-category of
continuous functors
\mathcal{C}_{1}\to\mathcal{C}_{2}
. In the proof of Theorem
4.29
,
we have used the following lemma:

Lemma 6.1
.

In the setting of Theorem
4.29
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Funct}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\operatorname{IndCoh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}),\operatorname{QCoh}(S^{\times d}))\simeq\operatorname{IndCoh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}\times S^{\times d}).

Here the right-hand side is the subcategory
with singular supports
\mathcal{N}\times S^{\times d}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}
is quasi-smooth and QCA of global complete intersection, the dg-category
\operatorname{IndCoh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})
is compactly generated by
\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})
, see
[
5
, Corollary 9.2.7]
.
Then from
[
17
, Section 1.1.1, 1.1.2]
, and noting

\operatorname{IndCoh}(S^{\times d})=\operatorname{QCoh}(S^{\times d})
as
S
is smooth,
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Funct}_{\mathrm{cont}}(\operatorname{IndCoh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}),\operatorname{QCoh}(S^{\times d}))

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{IndCoh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})^{\vee}\otimes\operatorname{IndCoh}(S^{\times d})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Ind}(\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})^{\mathrm{op}})\otimes\operatorname{IndCoh}(S^{\times d})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Ind}(\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}))\otimes\operatorname{IndCoh}(S^{\times d})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{IndCoh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})\otimes\operatorname{IndCoh}(S^{\times d})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{IndCoh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}\times S^{\times d}).

Here the 3rd equivalence is given by the derived dual

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}}\colon\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}})^{\mathrm{op}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}).

∎

6.6.
Another proof of Theorem
4.29

We give another proof that
\gamma
is an isomorphism in the proof of Theorem
4.29
, which works except when
g=2
and
G=\operatorname{GL}_{2}
.
The latter condition is equivalent to that

(6.15)

\displaystyle\operatorname{codim}(\mathcal{B}\setminus\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{ell}})=2(r-1)(g-1)-1\geqslant 2.

Proof.

Below we show that
\gamma
is an isomorphism except
g=2
and
G=\operatorname{GL}_{2}
.
Since
\Phi
is an equivalence over
\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{ell}}:=\mathrm{B}^{\mathrm{ell}}\cap\mathcal{B}
, the functor
\Upsilon
is zero over
\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{ell}}
. It follows that
\gamma
is an isomorphism over
\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{ell}}
. However the kernel object of
\mathrm{W}_{d,c}^{R}
is
(\mathcal{E}^{\vee}[1])^{\boxtimes d}
, which is a Cohen–Macaulay extension of a line bundle on

\displaystyle j\colon(\mathcal{C}\setminus\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sg}})^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}\subset\mathcal{C}^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}

up to shift, say
j_{*}^{\heartsuit}L
for a line bundle
L
and
j^{\heartsuit}=\mathcal{H}^{0}(j_{*})
. Therefore
\gamma
is given by an element

\displaystyle\gamma\in\operatorname{Hom}^{0}(j^{\heartsuit}_{*}L,j^{\heartsuit}_{*}L)

\displaystyle\cong\operatorname{Hom}^{0}(L,L)

\displaystyle\cong\mathcal{H}^{0}\Gamma(\mathcal{O}_{(\mathcal{C}\setminus\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sg}})^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}})

\displaystyle\cong\mathcal{H}^{0}\Gamma(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}})\cong\mathcal{H}^{0}\Gamma(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}}).

Here the 3rd isomorphism follows since
\mathcal{C}^{\times_{\mathcal{B}}d}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}
is normal (this follows from that it is smooth in codimension one and has at worst lci singularities; the latter is a consequence that it is both quasi-smooth and classical). The last isomorphism follows from
\mathcal{H}^{0}(\Gamma(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{C}}))=\mathcal{H}^{0}(\Gamma(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{H}(w)^{\mathrm{ss}}}))=\mathcal{H}^{0}\Gamma(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}})
. As
\gamma
is invertible over
\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{ell}}
, and the complement of
\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{ell}}
in
\mathcal{B}
is of codimension bigger than or equal to two by (
6.15
), it follows that
\gamma
is invertible over
\mathcal{B}
. Therefore
\gamma
is an isomorphism.
∎

6.7.
Quasi-BPS categories as semiorthogonal summands

We used the following lemma in the proof of Proposition
5.11
:

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
T^{\prime}
be a smooth scheme with a morphism

T^{\prime}\to T
such that
\omega_{T^{\prime}}
is trivial. For
any quasi-compact open substack

\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}}\subset\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi)
which admits a finite Harder-Narasimhan
stratification with an open immersion
j_{T}^{\mathrm{qc}}\colon\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi)^{\mathrm{ss}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}}
, the image
of

\displaystyle\operatorname{L}(\mathcal{H}_{T^{\prime}}(\chi)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{w^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle j_{T^{\prime}!}^{\mathrm{qc}}}}{{\to}}\operatorname{L}(\mathcal{H}_{T^{\prime}}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}})_{w^{\prime}}\subset\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}_{T^{\prime}}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}})

is a semiorthogonal summand of
\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}_{T^{\prime}}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}})
.

Proof.

There is a semiorthogonal
decomposition of the form

(6.16)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}_{T^{\prime}}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}})=\langle\ldots,\mathcal{W},\ldots\rangle,\ j_{T^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{qc}*}\colon\mathcal{W}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}_{T^{\prime}}(\chi)^{\mathrm{ss}})

by
[
21
, Theorem 3.3.1]
, which depends on a choice of
k_{\mathcal{Z}}\in\mathbb{R}

at each center of HN stratum
\mathcal{Z}
, and
\operatorname{L}(\mathcal{H}_{T^{\prime}}(\chi)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{w^{\prime}}

is a semiorthogonal summand of
\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}_{T}(\chi)^{\mathrm{ss}})
, see
[
32
, Theorem 1.1]
.

From the construction of
j_{T^{\prime}!}
, the image
\mathrm{Im}(j_{T^{\prime}!})

coincides with the image of the inclusion

\displaystyle\operatorname{L}(\mathcal{H}_{T^{\prime}}(\chi)^{\mathrm{ss}})_{w^{\prime}}\subset\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}_{T^{\prime}}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}})

determined by (
6.16
) for a choice

k_{\mathcal{Z}}=-c_{1}(\nu^{*}\det\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{H}_{T^{\prime}}(\chi)}^{\nu>0})/2
, where
\nu
is a map
\nu\colon B\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{H}_{T^{\prime}}(\chi)
as in Remark
2.15
.
Therefore the image of
j_{T^{\prime}!}^{\mathrm{qc}}
is
a semiorthogonal summand of

\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{H}_{T^{\prime}}(\chi)^{\mathrm{qc}})
.
∎
